Every scripted-walk site (`kNdsMenuWalk*` tables, `ndsMenuShellWalkTap`, `ndsMenuShellWalkWantsResultsStart`, the `NDS_P2_MENU_SHELL && NDS_P2_MENU_WALK` auto-START in `nds_platform.c:503`) is `#if NDS_P2_MENU_WALK`, so at 0 none of it exists in the translation unit — confirmed by `nm`: 0 hits for the full walk-symbol set on the free-play ELF against 17 hits on the walk-enabled `smash64ds-p2-shell-hwtri` control ELF from the same tree (positive control, same pattern). `ndsMenuShellReadTaps` falls straight through to `ndsPlatformReadInput`'s `scanKeys()/keysHeld()`, the same live keypad path `controller_backend.c`'s `osContGetReadData` uses for the battle this shell opens into. ROM: `builds/build-p2-shell-freeplay/smash64ds-p2-shell-freeplay-hwtri.nds`. Boot smoke (repo-local melonDS, `scripts/capture-melonds.ps1 -InGamePause`, a real ENTER keypress = Start per `emulators/melonds/melonDS.toml`'s `[Instance0.Keyboard] Start = 16777220`): title held 20 s free with zero input and no auto-advance, one injected START advanced Title → Mode Select, held another 12 s free and stayed on Mode Select unchanged — `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-19_p2-1l-freeplay-{title-idle20s,poststart,poststart-plus12s}.png`. Root ROMs unchanged (`576F51ED…E723`, `54C07FAC…C68A`). **Boundary waived by owner (2026-08-19), not run to completion:** the run was launched because the Makefile changed, passed every static/pre-build check (decomp pristine, M3 native stage, R2-03 fixed-point cubic, sprite lerp, MP floor-crossing, FTANIM surface/bake, particle banks, screen asset coverage, harness registry — all green) and was mid-way through the `p2_shell_loop` 20-lap arm when the owner waived the gate; abandoned there on instruction before either runtime arm (loop, mode-163 realtime) finished. The untracked root `smash64ds_P1.nds` (relocated to `builds/` for the run) was restored and hash-verified `576F51ED…E723` regardless, per the standing workaround — that step is not waivable and was completed. **(3) THE BLIT WAS NEVER WRONG — THE PANEL WAS SHOWING A STATE IT PASSES THROUGH.** The discriminating measurement is a gdb `dump binary memory` of BG2's bitmap (`bgInit(2, BgType_Bmp16, BgSize_B16_256x256, 0, 0)`, so base = VRAM+0) taken at the same `ndsPlatformEndFrame` stop as the photograph, compared against an offline evaluation of the same pose from the same table and the same rasters: **band rows 8..157, ZERO differing texels** at the presents whose read timing lines up, while the photograph of that stop shows the black drop-shadow cutout alone with no letters. Two independent confirmations that the table is not the suspect: the pose table linked into both shell ELFs is byte-identical to `src/nds/generated/mn_title_anim.generated.inc` (255 entries, 0 mismatches), and an offline run of all 51 poses draws non-zero opaque texels on every pose but pose 1, whose five table entries are legitimately all zero-width. **The mechanism is the live scanout.** The layer is scanned out one row every ~2,130 ticks while the CPU writes it, and the old draw was erase-the-whole-dirty-rectangle then five top-to-bottom passes, the first of them the unkeyed cutout. Beam and passes both sweep downward, so every band row has a window where the beam has passed what the cutout pass reached but not what the letter passes reached — displayed BLACK — and an earlier one between the erase and the cutout — displayed EMPTY. Measured peak 712,704 ticks against a 560,190-tick sweep: 1.27 sweeps, so no pose table can avoid it. **FIX, at the owning seam** (`ndsUiKitTitleAnimDraw`, `src/nds/nds_ui_kit.c`): a destination row is composed WHOLE in the existing scaled-row buffer — cleared, then the pieces covering it in the source's own draw order — and reaches the layer in one left-to-right run of aligned word stores. Whatever the beam catches is either the previous pose's row or this pose's row, never a half-built one. The separate erase is folded away (a row no piece covers composes to zeros, and zero IS the erase) and the union rectangle is stored once instead of an erase plus five overlapping piece rectangles, so it is also LESS work: **peak pose cost 712,704 → 651,904 ticks (−8.5%), title window `v2` 4 → 1 two-VBlank presents over the same 38, `max=2` unchanged, steady state after the settle `v1=129 v2=1 … max=2`**. The row cache the (d) row measured is kept, moved up one level: when none of the five pieces' source rows moved, the composed line is already correct and only the store runs. **ONE DRAFT DEFECT, CAUGHT BY THE SAME INSTRUMENT AND WORTH RECORDING:** the first composer unioned the previous dirty rectangle with the new pose's, and `kNdsUiKitTitleAnimRects[0]` is `{0,0,0,0}` — an EMPTY rect, not a rect at the origin — so the union reached row 0 and cleared the (f2) upper border band. The layer dump named it exactly: band rows still matched the reference, rows 0..7 held 0 texels against the bake's 1,702. `ndsUiKitTitleAnimUnion` now skips zero-area rectangles AND clamps to `[NDS_MN_TITLE_ANIM_TOP, NDS_MN_TITLE_ANIM_BOTTOM)`, so a future table or dirty-rectangle mistake cannot destroy the two edge-anchored bands. **STANDING GUARD:** `gNdsUiKitTitleAnimEmptyPoseCount` — the composer ORs every word it stores, so a pose that stored only zeros costs one compare to detect — asserted `empty=0` by `verify-p2-shell-loop.ps1` over all twenty laps and printed by `capture-p2-1k-title-anim.ps1` (`empty=0` at presents 16/17/18). **(1) THE RED ARTIFACT IS THE EMBLEM, AND ITS PLACEMENT WAS RIGHT.** `mnTitleMakeLogoNoOpening` (mntitle.c:1051) is this build's branch, sets the SObj primitive to pure red with `SP_TRANSPARENT`, and `mnTitleLogoProcDisplay` (:1011) draws it through a FLAT combiner at `sMNTitleLogoAlpha = 0x4C` from tic 170 (:374): a 30% red wash over its whole silhouette, fire included. A DS 16-bit BG bitmap carries one alpha bit, so the bake could keep it only where an opaque texel lay beneath — measured by rebuilding TITLE_SCREEN with and without it: **1,243 texels differ, bbox x 160..235 y 0..96, of which 179 are rows 0..6 of the (f2) border band at x 202..236** (near-black RGB5 (2,1,0) → (11,1,0)), the rest scattered tint on the wordmark. That 179-texel block is the owner's report. Not an (f2) side effect: the emblem's own raster spans DS rows −2..96, so it covers the band at its 4/5 position (362 texels over rows 8..15) as well as at (f2)'s (179 over rows 0..7). Omitted from `TITLE_PARTS` and from `TITLE_WORDMARK`, delta recorded in `mn_screen_coverage_allowlist.json` as **status `open` — owner ruling requested**, because reinstating the wash faithfully needs a layer the DS can alpha-blend (a semi-transparent OBJ, DS OBJ mode 1 with BLDALPHA, over the 102x99 silhouette). Side effect, measured: TITLE_WORDMARK shrinks 238x111@(18,0) → 219x72@(18,39), **21,300 fewer resident bytes** in the title scene's arena block, and the settled rectangle no longer reaches rows the border band owns. Proof: 412 dark-red pixels in the top band before (window x 324..370 = DS x 202..232), **0** after. **(2) IS NOT EITHER FULL-BLEED BAND** — that half held, and still does. Both named suspects were measured full-width in the bake AND on the shipped ROM: the upper border band is opaque x 0..255 on rows 0..2 and then tapers by the ART's own shape (left gap ≥ right gap on every tapered row, the opposite of the report), and the copyright band is opaque x 0..255 on rows 163..191. **THE "MOST LIKELY IS (3)" HALF IS RETRACTED** (2026-08-19): the owner re-checked on the repaired ROM and (2) was still there. The torn poses' cutout rectangle really is asymmetric left-vs-right, and that is exactly the trap — it matched the WORDS of the report ("gaps left, more right") while the element the owner was looking at was never on BG2 at all. **A suspect that reproduces the phrasing of a report is not a suspect that reproduces its pixels; localize the bbox before naming the surface.** The true mechanism is the fire, in the (2) entry below. **(11)** New flag `NDS_BOOT_DIAG_TEXT` (default 1, `override … := 0` in the free-play block only) guards the three `iprintf` boot lines; the three globals behind them (`gNdsRelocAssetInitResult`, `gNdsBootSelfTestResult`, `gNdsOriginalBootStage`) are untouched and every verifier still reads them over gdb. **RESIDUAL, STATED:** a single horizontal seam between two consecutive poses can still appear on the heaviest frames — inherent to a single-buffered live-scanned layer, and each row is now a valid pose. Removing it entirely needs either a second 96 KB VRAM bitmap (the bank layout has 32 KB free at each of two holes, so it does not fit) or a main-RAM shadow of the union DMA'd in (≈76 KB, against a heap low-water already under the GObj-cap threshold) — an owner/architecture call, not a bake call. **CAPTURES** `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-19_p2-1L-title-{before-pose36,before-pose38,before-pose41,after-pose34,after-pose36,after-pose40,after-pose18-peak,after-pose20-peak,after-settled}.png` **(5)(6)(7)(8) — ONE DEFECT CLASS AND ONE OF ITS OWN.** (5) and (6) are the same mistake at two sizes: **an OBJ-cell ratio applied to LAYOUT art.** (7) is a rounding, (8) is the (f)/(f2) full-bleed ruling reaching two more edges. Every root cause was found offline from the bake before a ROM was built, and each has its own falsifiable measurement. **(7) THE PLATE'S BLACK BREAKS WERE NINETEEN INDEPENDENT ROUNDINGS.** `mnMapsMakeLabels` (mnmaps.c:439) draws the 4x20 `llMNMapsPlateMiddleSprite` at every x from 186 to 258 step 4, so the source plate is contiguous to the pixel — left cap 174..185 (12 wide), middle 186..261, right cap 262..285 (24 wide), no overlap and no gap. The bake placed all nineteen tiles SEPARATELY at the frame's 4/5, where a 4 px tile rounds to 3 px while the step between tiles is 3.2, so the art falls one pixel behind the layout every five tiles. Measured by recompositing SSS_SCREEN with the three plate placements removed and comparing columns: **DS columns 161, 177, 193 and 209 of rows 153..168 were byte-identical to the plateless backdrop** — four one-pixel holes through to the stone, exactly the owner's report. FIX at the owning seam: the middle is ONE tiled run, `tile=(76,20) period=(4,None)` — the same `tile`/`period` the VS option-tab middle has used since P2-1j, because an RDP wrap is a continuous fill and not a row of stamps — resampled once onto 149..209, flush against 139..148 and 210..228. It does not breach the P2 stability requirement for a 60 Hz screen (99.94% single-VBlank) and no work was added to any path this row owns; **the next cycle on this screen should name that frame** rather than re-derive the A/B. **(9) THE PREVIEW PANEL'S ART IS THE STAGE'S OWN 300x220 BACKGROUND, AND OURS WAS THE 30x23 GRID ICON.** `mnMapsMakePreviewWallpaper` (mnmaps.c:909) builds the panel from three things and only the third is per-stage: `gDPFillRectangle(43,130,152,211)` at black alpha 0x73 (:899), seven 16x82 `llMNMapsTilesSprite` at every x from 43 to 139 (:919) — both already in `SSS_SCREEN` since P2-1k — and then **`sMNMapsGroundInfo->wallpaper` at scale 0.37 at (40,127)** (:956), or `llMNMapsRandomBigSprite` (110x82) at the same site UNSCALED for RANDOM (:931). P2-1f substituted the 48x36 grid icon at 5/8 for that third element, which is the owner's "a placeholder not filling its box, distinct from the icon". **THE ART WAS FOUND BY DEREFERENCE, NOT BY NAME.** `sMNMapsGroundInfo` is the `MPGroundData` at `llGRPupupuMapMapHeader`, whose `wallpaper` field (mptypes.h:182, +0x48) is a POINTER — so no `&ll<Name>Sprite` reference to it exists in any `mn*` source, which is why no bake had found it and why the coverage audit reports it as an `extra` whose allowlist entry explains the indirection rather than excusing an addition. One discriminating read settled it: `reloc_stages/GRPupupuMap` is a **290-byte header stub** (a `RELO` whose declared payload exceeds the file), the payload lives in the sibling `reloc_stages/StageDreamLand`, and `llStageDreamLandSprite` there decodes as **300x220 RGBA16, 44 bands of 6 rows stepping 5** — the exact band geometry `decode_sprite_raster` already transcribes from spDraw, and the same symbol `reloc_backend_assets.c:8268` resolves for the battle. **FIX AT THE BAKE, ONE EXACT FRACTION rather than a scale followed by a rounding:** `O2R_DIRS` gains `reloc_stages`, and two new surfaces composited over `SSS_BACKGROUND` share the declared box `(40,127,111,82)` — 111 = 300 x 0.37, the art's own scaled width, and 82 = the RANDOM sprite's own height, so a Dream Land ↔ RANDOM move overwrites the previous state exactly. `SSS_PREVIEW_DREAM_LAND` takes `scale=(37,125)` (0.37 x 4/5 composed, ONE resample 300x220 → **89x65**) and `SSS_PREVIEW_RANDOM` the frame's plain 4/5 (110x82 → **88x66**), which is the source's own split: the RANDOM arm is the one branch that never touches `sobj->sprite.scalex`, and it is why `attr &= ~SP_FASTCOPY` (:958) sits on the other branch alone — a fast-copy blit cannot scale. **BOX FILL, MEASURED OFFLINE ON THE COMPOSITED SCREEN** (every texel inside the panel's own 89x66 box that differs from the plateless backdrop): **690/5,874 = 11.7% before, bbox (36,103)..(65,125)** — a 30x23 picture in an 89x66 box, and identically 690 for both states because both 5/8 icons are fully opaque — against **5,785/5,874 = 98.5% after for Dream Land, bbox (32,102)..(120,166)** and **5,808/5,874 = 98.9% for RANDOM, bbox (32,102)..(119,167)**. The ~1.5% residual is the source's own relationship, not a shortfall: the art is 89x65 and 88x66 inside an 89x66 box exactly as the source's 111x81.4 and 110x82 sit inside a 112x82 tile field. **THE PANEL STOPS BEING AN OBJ, AND THAT EVICTS THREE CELLS.** 89x66 is past every DS OBJ cell, so it is a surface — and its removal took the last consumer of `MAP_DREAM_LAND` and `MAP_RANDOM` (kept in the pack by (6) *only* for this panel) and of `PORTRAIT_LOCKED` (the eviction candidate `docs/p2/P2-1c-vram-map.md` named at P2-1j, whose only remaining namer was `ndsMenuShellSssCellImage`'s unreachable locked branch). **Image block 45,696 → 39,552 B, 35 → 32 images, bank E floor 3,456 → 9,600 B** — and the 5/8 ratio leaves the tree with them, so nothing on either select screen is at any ratio but 4/5. `NDS_SSS_ICON_INSET_X/Y` and the depth-ordered sprite-slot list are deleted; the cursor is the only OBJ the stage select still owns. **ONE SURFACE A FRAME, BECAUSE P2-1j ALREADY PRICED THE ALTERNATIVE.** A Dream Land ↔ RANDOM move changes BOTH the plaque (8,162 B) and the preview (11,748 B) — the same burst that cost the VS menu 606,336 ticks on one frame — so `ndsMenuShellSssSyncSurfaces(budget)` is `ndsMenuShellVsSyncButtons`' own shape: the move frame spends ZERO (it already carries the FGM cue), the per-frame update spends one, and only the screen ENTRY spends both. **CADENCE, SAME-TREE A/B AGAINST THE (5)(6)(7)(8) ARM** (`scripts/menus/probe-p2-shell.ps1`, `smash64ds-p2-shell-hwtri`, fast logic 0; `artifacts/verification/2026-08-19_p2-shell.txt` → `…_p2-shell-p2-1L-9.txt`): **every screen's VBlank histogram is IDENTICAL** — title `149 1 0 0 max=2`, main menu `301 0 0 0 max=1`, VS rules `1811 0 0 0 max=1`, character select `1611 1 0 0 max=2`, **stage select `244 0 0 0 max=1`, every present single-VBlank**. pixel-exact offline pair `2026-08-19_p2-1L-preview9-{before,after}-sss-{default,random}.png` (surface + OBJ composited at the runtime's own coordinates, 3x). **REFRESHED FREE-PLAY ROM** for the owner: `builds/build-p2-shell-freeplay/smash64ds-p2-shell-freeplay-hwtri.nds`, SHA256 `BAD5EB4DA622D396154B7C8850A954D08CBBEA046819DBD7CFDA3CEF504B79C4`. **(4) PARKED — `BLOCKED(decision: does the character select stay a 2D shell screen, or become an imported scene?)`, AND THE CHEAP PATH'S PREMISE IS REFUTED BY THE SOURCE.** The premise offered for a pre-rendered sprite was that the CSS preview is a fixed idle pose. It is not. `mnPlayersVSFighterProcUpdate` (mnplayersvs.c:1582) adds **+2.0° of Y rotation every tic** to an unselected fighter — a continuous turntable, one revolution per 180 tics (3 s) — and on confirm spins it at **+20°/tic** until it wraps to zero and then plays a WIN demo animation (`mnPlayersVSGetStatusSelected` :1553: Mario `nFTDemoStatusWin3`, Fox `nFTDemoStatusWin4`). `mnPlayersVSMakeFighter` (:1624) is a full `ftManagerMakeFighter` with its own `figatree_heap`, placed at `x = player*840 − 1250, y = −850` at `dSCSubsysFighterScales[fkind]`, under its own camera (:1666, eye z 5000, DL links 18/15/10/9). So a static pose is a still where the source turns — an approximation of exactly what the owner asked for, and the row was told not to ship one silently. **PRICES, ALL MEASURED IN-TREE.** *CPU:* the current per-fighter render cost is **Mario 173,248/174,848 and Fox 213,280/213,632 P50/P95 ticks per frame** (`docs/PERF_LEDGER.md:4961`, the most recent synchronized A/B; combined 385,312/388,480). The character select's own worst frame is **586,176** (this cycle's probe) against 560,190 at 60 Hz and 1,120,380 at the owner-sanctioned 30 Hz. So at 30 Hz: one fighter 759,424 (68% of two VBlanks), two 972,704 (87%), **four 1,439,296 (128% — three VBlanks, i.e. 20 Hz)**. Four panels of live fighter do not fit two VBlanks; one or two do. *RAM is NOT the blocker:* `LOOPARENA size=1544192` and the arena rewinds per scene entry, and `nSCKindVSResults` already carries live fighters at a **1,322,828 B** high-water against the character select's present **126,704 B** — so the arena holds it. What it costs is a per-entry fighter asset load (MarioModel 30,056 B + FoxModel 32,424 B of reloc payload, plus animations), and `PROJECT_GOAL.md` rules loading time cheap. *Architecture, and this is the decision:* **the port ALREADY renders live 3D fighters on a non-battle screen** — Results is not a shell screen at all but the imported `mnVSResultsFuncRun` (`nds_menu_shell.c:551`, `src/import/battleship_mnvsresults.c`, `ndsFighterManagerRegisterDisplayFighter` on the source's own fighter DL link 9), while the character select is a hand-written 2D loop. The faithful mechanism is therefore not new plumbing, it is a choice of SHAPE for the CSS — and that choice is the owner's. *The 2D alternative is not the cheap one either:* a faithful turntable at even a coarse 15° step is 24 sprites per fighter per costume, ~48x64 at 16 bpp ≈ 147,456 B of ROM each (cheap) but **6,144 B of BG2 traffic per panel per update**; at 4 panels x 30 Hz that is 24,576 B/frame, which at this cycle's measured **19.1 ticks/byte** is roughly **469,000 ticks/frame** against the 534,204 ticks of 30 Hz headroom the CSS has. It fits only at 15° granularity against the source's 2°, needs the whole sprite pipeline built, and is still a stepped still. **NOTHING WAS SHIPPED FOR (4)**, by the rail that a fidelity/architecture call is reported and never chosen. **Round-6 (owner, 2026-08-19): RULINGS — (4) live 3D on the shell CSS, like the original (2 slots now; the 4-fighter 128% figure is P2-2 inheritance); emblem red wash REMOVE (ruled, allowlist entry closes); title input gate = press START immediately (ruled, current behavior stands); tearing seam explained to owner, stands as accepted delta. BUGS — (b1) menu/CSS/SSS BGM loop seam AUDIBLE: track "ends then begins again" instead of continuous loop — investigate loop-point mechanics end-to-end (render loop_start/loop_end vs source CSEQ markers, refill wrap path, underrun at seam); (2) FIXED green (2026-08-19, second attribution) — **THE ELEMENT IS THE FIRE ITSELF**, not the wordmark composite, not either band, not the torn poses. **LOCALIZED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS TOUCHED:** over rows 8..157 the only two column steps that reproduce across three independent title captures are x=5\|6 and x=240\|241 (freeplay-idle20s / title-after-settled / preview9-rom-title = 17.9/22.4/28.3 and 16.7/24.5/20.4 luma); every other strong step moves with the fire art between captures. Both strips read 25–56 luma BRIGHTER than the interior, i.e. one of the two fire layers was missing there. **MECHANISM:** the title's fire SObjs are display link 0 (mntitle.c:945) and the camera that renders links 0+1 is `mnTitleMakeCameras`' sprite camera, viewport `syRdpSetViewport(&cobj->viewport, 10, 10, 310, 230)` (:1413) — the same 300x220 active area P2-1k (f) gave every other background — and the nearer SObj's own texture rectangle, source x [8, 312) y [8, 232), CONTAINS that viewport on all four sides, so **the source has no gap anywhere**. `build_fire_atlas` was mapping the whole 320x240 FRAME instead (step 320/51), dragging the frame's out-of-viewport margin onto a DS panel that has no overscan to eat it; and `_fire_sample` rejected `u > 31` — the last texel CENTRE — instead of the rectangle's `u >= 32`, dropping one whole texel column and row of every layer (9.5 source px on the nearer one). Together: the nearer layer absent from screen columns **0..5 and 241..255**, left 6 px and right 15 px, "gaps on the left and even more on the right" to the column. **FIX:** map the atlas to the camera's active area (`FIRE_VIEW_*`) and test coverage against the rectangle. Both edits are load-bearing — the viewport mapping alone still leaves columns 251..255 and rows 188..191. **PROOF, bake A/B on identical reloc data:** x=5\|6 was **rank 1 of 255** column steps in BOTH fire cells (18.26 / 29.14) and is now 2.03 / 0.23 (rank 150 / 209); x=240\|241 was 14.17 / 19.18, now 0.05 / 1.99; the column profile at the boundary goes from a flat 221.0 plateau dropping to 191.9 in one column, to 190.3 → 190.0. Coverage arithmetic: **0** missing atlas columns and rows for both layers, against 4 and 4. **CONFINED:** of the 73 baked surfaces exactly three previews differ (the atlas and the two title composites that embed it) — 70 byte-identical; surface pack unchanged at 1,161,560 B, so zero ROM cost, and no runtime change at all (FIRE_PA/FIRE_PD and the blit path are untouched — only what each atlas texel SAMPLES moved). **AND THE LAYER DUMP SETTLES IT INDEPENDENTLY OF THE BAKE:** `-DumpLayer` on the settled title (present140) shows BG2 holding **0** opaque texels in x 0..5 and **0** in x 241..255 over rows 8..157, opaque extent 22..235 — nothing our shell draws was ever in those strips, so the element could only have been BG3, i.e. the fire. That same extent is what refutes the round-5 prime suspect on its own terms: TITLE_WORDMARK's box is 18..236, a left gap of 18 and a right gap of 19, near-SYMMETRIC and not the reported asymmetry. **ON-PANEL PROOF:** three settled captures (pose 51, three different fire cells 19/3/5) — the x=5\|6 step is 0.01/3.13/1.54 (rank 220/99/157 of 255) and x=240\|241 is 0.83/4.82/0.55 (rank 190/63/194), against rank 1/2/1 and 2/1/4 on the three before-captures; and the fire ANIMATES through the former plate, mean \|Δ\| between fire cells 28.72/31.50/15.89 in x0..5 and 14.71/18.31/15.60 in x241..255 versus 14.03..14.67 in the interior. BUG (b1) FIXED green (2026-08-19): two independent mechanisms, both in collect_loop_metadata() (scripts/sfx/bgm/render-audio-bgm-pupupu.py) -- (i) Mode Select only: the old code took max() over every CSEQ channel's raw loop-marker tick with no regard for per-channel loop PERIOD (n_env.c __n_alCSeqGetTrackEvent, independently re-decoded); 3 channels shared a 7680-tick period but 4 near-silent outlier channels (two with 1-2 notes total) carried four different, much longer periods, and the longest outlier dominated max(), splicing a loud unrelated moment onto dead silence every loop, loop_start ~14s from the tune's own phrase; (ii) all four looping tracks: the render appended a flat ~1s trailing-silence pad that landed inside the loop region, measured offline as true digital silence (RMS 0.0) in the 10ms before the wrap, most audible on Battle Select's short ~13s loop. Fix groups channels by loop period (mode wins ties toward shortest), anchors on the majority group's own latest entry (empirically best of the candidates -- 0ms dead air at the seam vs ~600ms for the others, from rendered-PCM seam comparison), walks to the period boundary covering the mix's last real note, and truncates the stream there instead of the flat pad; provably a no-op for Pupupu/Results/Battle Select's loop_start (every channel already agreed on one period). Re-rendered and re-pinned all six BGM tracks (include/nds/nds_audio_bgm.h, scripts/check-audio-bgm-derived-assets.ps1; Win Mario/Win Fox byte-identical, they do not loop). Verified: check-audio-bgm-derived-assets.ps1 GREEN; `.\scripts\verify-all.ps1 -Profile Boundary` GREEN both arms (root ROMs `smash64ds.nds` 54C07FAC...C68A and `smash64ds-battle-playable-hwtri.nds`/`smash64ds_P1.nds` 576F51ED...E723 all byte-identical before/after); melonDS hold >=2 wraps clean on both (Mode Select loop=38, Battle Select loop=34, all fail counters 0, posbytes mapped inside the new loop region). Fix: unroll_channel_loops() replicates each channel through its own period to the mix loop_end. | P2-3r1 | Mario/Luigi entry pipe: wrong colour, body missing | **green — owner visual pass pending** | **Two defects in the native entry owner, both measured against the source before touching it.** (1) The baked pipe vertices carry unit NORMALS in r,g,b (`146,0,192` = (−110,0,−64), norm 127): the pipe is a LIT model (TEXEL0×SHADE combine, `G_LIGHTING` inherited from `scVSBattleFuncLights`, its own `gSPLightColor` words white/0x99 at 0x3c8-0x3e0), and the owner masked `G_LIGHTING` off and emitted the normals as colours — green texture × g=0 = black. The generator's "no lighting" assertion was vacuous because its walker started the geometry mode at 0. Fix: the generator now tracks set AND cleared geometry bits (`geometry_clear`), decodes the lists' `G_MW_LIGHTCOL` words into the group row (`light_color_1/2`, `light_mask`) and refuses a direction/count word; the runtime resolves `(initial & ~clear) | set`, seeds the battle light direction from the fighter display contract (same seed as the stage traversal) and the effect's MObj/fighter material colours, and shades lit groups through `ndsRendererHardwareLitShadeColorPrepared` like the native fighter owner. Census: all 30 groups inherit `G_LIGHTING`; the Arwing's one SHADE-combined group (root 0x2a20, real normals) was equally wrong and is fixed by the same change. (2) "Body missing" was not a defect: the source AnimJoint (decoded at asset 356 +0x6d8) scales the tube 1e-05→1 over 7 frames, raises it 14→321 over 18, holds 50, lowers 18, shrinks — and Mario's Appear motion lifts TopN 16.8/frame to exactly 420 = 321−21+120, the cap. The first captures looked like "cap only at ground level" because the capture harness's `-EntrySeries` steps are 60 Hz logic frames, so `+32` is source frame 32 at the hold, with the 120-unit tube hanging under the cap. **EVIDENCE:** `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-22_pipe-final-fighter-entry-1{,+14,+24,+40,+64}.png` — frame 8 pipe grown, 14 Mario rising inside it, 24 standing on the cap with the green body below, 64 landed with the pipe gone and Fox's Arwing arriving. **Tooling fixed on the way:** `capture-p2-shell.ps1` arms `BreakOnStartup` (two full-timeout runs attached after `ndsMenuShellRunTitle` had already run once), gains `-Only` and `-EntrySeries`, and works on a boot-into-battle lab ROM; `docs/VERIFYING.md` carries the step-unit law. **Measurement lesson recorded in memory:** on the cache-accurate melonDS fork, gdb reads of hot stack memory return stale RAM — three probes reported a "garbage" modelview that the CPU never saw. **Observed, not attributed (owner's eye needed):** Fox's Arwing body draws dark grey with a red/green glow (`2026-08-22_pipe-final-fighter-entry-1+64.png`, top-left); its 0x1fa0 root inherits `G_LIGHTING` like the pipe but carries white vertex colours that are not normals, and it looked the same before this row. Decide against the N64 before touching it. |
| P2-3r2 | "Luigi has issues" — owner specifics (2026-08-23): "last time I played Luigi, there were no animations" | **fixed 2026-08-23 — owner play pass pending** | **Root cause: `lbCommonPlayTranslateScaledDObjAnim` was an empty stub in `reloc_backend_compat_shims.c`.** Luigi's attributes carry `translate_scales` (he plays Mario's figatrees at his own limb lengths), so `ftParamUpdateAnimKeys` (ft/ftparam.c:364) routes EVERY one of his joints through that player instead of `gcPlayDObjAnimJoint`; the parser still ran (anim_wait counted down, AObj tracks were written) but nothing ever evaluated a track into `rotate`/`translate`, so the pose never moved. Mario/Fox never reach the scaled player, which is why P1/P2-2 could not see it. Evidence: new `scripts/probe-fighter-anim-state.ps1` (breaks in `ftParamUpdateAnimKeys`, dumps FTStruct identity, figatree attach counters and every common joint's wait/frame/script/rot/tra, `-AObjs` adds the Q-word track dump) read Luigi at status 10 motion 4 with `wait` decrementing per tick and every joint `rot=0,0,0` two ticks apart while Fox's moved (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-23_fighter-anim-state.txt` holds the post-fix run). Fix: lb/lbcommon.c:1261 ported as the one shared player body — `gcPlayDObjAnimJoint` and the scaled player are the same `always_inline` body with a `tra_scale` that the unscaled wrapper passes as constant NULL (the ITCM player stays 0x25c bytes; without `always_inline` GCC outlined the body to main RAM and left a 10-byte ITCM tail call). Post-fix probe: Luigi joint 8 rot 3.3745→3.3520 across two ticks, joint 11 translate y 89.91 = Mario's 84 × his scale. The per-joint clock stays the source's f32 DObj fields with the source's arithmetic, so motion-script frame counts are bit-identical. **The hold** (`NDS_FT_POSE_HOLD`): body joints are evaluated on the last source tick of a presented frame and on the attach tick; TransN/XRotN/YRotN (the materialised hidden parts, whose delta is velocity — `ftphysics.c:172`) every tick. It is LAZY: a held joint runs only its clock (no pool line touched) and the next evaluation adds `speed × held` to every track, with the parser folding the held count out of any segment it starts mid-hold and into the End tail, so the evaluated phase is the source's exactly. **Proof** (`NDS_FT_POSE_ORACLE`, shadow DObj/GObj per joint compared bit for bit after every tick, `scripts/probe-ft-pose-oracle.ps1`): Luigi-vs-Fox lab, 176,469 joint compares over 162 attaches — **0 mismatches** without the hold (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-23_ft-pose-oracle.txt`) and **0 mismatches** with it (60,101 held joint-ticks, `…-oracle-hold.txt`; the one accepted representation difference is a held End keeping the END sentinel one tick where the source's player had already written NULL — same clock, and the oracle names it). The first hold attempt read 1,490 mismatches, all the End tail missing the held adds — found by the oracle, fixed, re-proven. **Memory**: the first stress run breached the 25,600 B heap floor (15,072) with 28-byte tracks in fixed per-joint slots; the pool layout plus `NDS_R2_AOBJ_POOL_COUNT` 512→384 under the engine (live AObj peak with the engine on: 297, `…ft-pose-fourcpu-counters.txt`) reads low-water 32,224 (engine) / 32,540 (hold). **Four-CPU stress** (`verify-p2-four-fighter-stress.ps1 -Build …`): engine alone `WORK-H` P50/P95 **1,270,144 / 1,823,296** — flat against P2-2p5's 1,264,512 / 1,836,800, because the per-PC census (`artifacts/performance/2026-08-23_p2-2p6-fourcpu-profile128-pose/`) puts the engine at the same ~115K tk/fr the AObj path cost (`ndsFtPosePlay` 73K, the inlined parse 42K): per evaluated track the Hermite is ~36 cycles and the rest is DObj/pool cache misses, the Q→f32 store and the soft-float clock, which a representation change does not delete. With the lazy hold: **1,251,328 / 1,795,776** (SINT 320K → 288K P50), gate checks green, VBI 2/3/4/5+ = 357/1382/202/32. **The Q12 clock landed** (`3fb8645ebd6`): joint `wait_q`/`frame_q` replace the per-joint f32 soft-float clock, `speed_q` converts once per update, the GObj frame publishes once per run, and the DObj f32 fields keep only the source sentinels plus a RUNNING marker — oracle **0 mismatches over 189,251 compares** (four samples, hold on; `artifacts/verification/2026-08-23_ft-pose-oracle.txt`), stress **1,244,608 / 1,777,408** (−6,720 P50 / −18,368 P95 vs the hold arm; SINT 288K → 261,248; heap free min 31,660; VBI 2/3/4/5+ 367/1378/193/35, all gates green). Cumulative P2-2p6: 1,264,512 / 1,836,800 → **1,244,608 / 1,777,408** (−19,904 / −59,392). Remaining in the lane, next: walk #1 of `ndsFTParamsInvalidateSubtree` skipped on ticks where no joint was evaluated (~8K); then the soft-float caller census (`census-softfloat-callers.ps1` on the hold ROM) decides the next lane — `__aeabi_fadd`+`fmul`+`fdiv` are 20.3M+13.3M+4.7M cycles of the 128-frame profile (~150K tk/fr) and the animation lane is no longer their owner. |

| P2-3r4 | Boundary static check RED since `a0f488256e4` (2026-08-22, DK agent) | **fixed 2026-08-23** | `check-harness-registry.ps1` pinned the realtime loop harness's `$marioTrianglesInBattle`/`% 320` text; the DK session generalised fighter 0 to `$p0*` with `$p0OwnerTriangles` (320 outside a P2 production proof) and did not update the check, so every `verify-all -Profile Boundary` since then threw before running an arm. The check now pins the generalised shape including the `else { 320 }` Mario contract. Found by the first Boundary on the packet-promoted tree. | **PROGRESS 2026-08-24, and a correction to this row's own arithmetic.** Slices 1-2 are landed: the generator emits each P2-3 owner+detail as one struct image (layout stays the compiler's, so image bytes and the runtime's `offsetof` cannot drift), the five ungated element types moved to `include/nds/nds_native_fighter_tables.h` so there is ONE definition, and the build compiles each image standalone, lifts `.fighter_image` with `objcopy` and stages it under `nitrofs/fighters/` behind the same ships-only-with-its-reader rule the UI kit uses. Measured images: luigi high 8,596 / low 5,964, donkey high 10,952 / low 7,324 -- 32,836 B of ARM9 binary. **THE NET IS NOT 32,836 B, and the earlier note in this row read as if it were.** The buffers the runtime loads into come out of the same arena the binary was starving, so the honest sum is: capacity +32,836 (arrays gone) minus whatever a match actually loads. Only the kinds in play need an image and only one detail level is live at a time (High at <=2 fighters, Low at 3-4), so a Mario-vs-DK match spends 10,952 and nets about +21,900; a Mario/Fox match loads none and nets the full +32,836. Against roster-2's measured 13,840 B that is still decisive, and unlike the arrays it stops growing with the roster: twelve fighters would be ~200 KB of image data against a pool bounded by four slots. **Next slice:** the loader and the per-kind tables struct filled from the loaded image, then delete the in-binary arrays for image owners (they must go in the SAME change that starts reading the image, so the two can never disagree), then flip roster 2 and re-measure headroom.
| P2-3r4 | Per-fighter native-owner tables move to NitroFS (the DK unblock) | **DONE 2026-08-24 (`8ab609d62a6`, `e1a206991a5`); roster 2 shipped, Boundary green on three arms** | **THE BUDGET LAW IS WHAT BLOCKS DONKEY KONG, and this is the row that pays it.** P2_PLAN law 3 says a unit that busts its budget is not landed, and risk 2 names arena headroom exactly. Measured (2026-08-23, one build per arm, read at the battle's own high water): roster 0 headroom 91,664 B, roster 1 (+Luigi) 57,136 B, roster 2 (+Donkey Kong) 13,840 B and the battle takes a data abort in `ifCommonCountdownMakeInterface` when the countdown interface's taskman allocation returns NULL. Arena USED is identical across all three; only CAPACITY moves, because the arena is calloc'd from what the heap has left and every byte of ARM9 binary costs it 1:1. **Where the bytes are:** `sNdsNative*Fighter*` tables total 87,576 B in the roster-1 shell ELF -- 64,147 B of frozen Mario/Fox and 23,429 B of Luigi -- and DK's set is the ~45 KB that pushes it over. Ten more fighters at this rate is 400+ KB of binary that no DS RAM budget can hold, so this is a scaling wall, not a DK-specific one. **The seam is already right:** the renderer reaches every owner's geometry through one pointer-table struct (`NDSNativeFighterRuntimeTables`, selected by `ndsRendererNativeSelectFighterRuntimeTables`), so if those pointers address a loaded buffer instead of `.rodata`, no renderer code changes. **Slice:** (a) `generate_nds_native_owners.py` emits, per P2-3 owner and detail level, one NitroFS blob with a header of array offsets plus the C filler that maps offsets to the struct's fields; (b) the fighter setup path loads the blob for the kinds a match actually uses into a per-slot buffer and fills the struct at load time (PROJECT_GOAL: loading time is cheap, gameplay CPU is precious); (c) Mario/Fox keep their frozen byte-identical export per the P2-3 plan's own bootstrap contract; (d) a checker pins blob/struct agreement the way `check-fighter-production-manifest.ps1` pins the manifest. **Acceptance:** DK builds without `NDS_P2_SHELL_ROSTER_ALLOW_OVERFLOW`, the roster-2 battle reaches GAME SET, arena headroom at the battle high water is >= the roster-1 figure (57,136 B), and Boundary stays green on three arms. **HOW IT LANDED.** Each P2-3 owner's twenty-one generated arrays are now one struct, compiled standalone, `objcopy`d into `nitrofs/fighters/<owner>_<detail>.bin`, and guarded OUT of the ARM9 binary by `NDS_NATIVE_OWNER_IMAGE_<OWNER>`; the runtime tables struct is filled at fighter creation from the loaded image. The layout stays the compiler's, so the image bytes and the runtime's `offsetof` cannot drift. The two generators that must agree about WHICH arrays moved cannot import each other, so the list lives in `scripts/fighters/native_owner_image_arrays.py` and each asserts its own view against it -- disagreement is silent in both directions (an array in both places pays arena for nothing; an array in neither is a dangling table pointer in a draw path). **The proof is a build, not an argument:** `NDS_NATIVE_OWNER_IMAGE=0 NDS_NATIVE_OWNER_IMAGE_VERIFY=1` keeps the arrays, loads the image anyway and compares them member by member at fighter creation -- measured on the shell target, roster 1, one scripted lap: `load=4 fail=0 bytes=32152 match=160 mismatch=0`. **Measured outcome:** binary text 1,111,776 -> 1,096,048 for Luigi alone; roster 2 arena headroom 13,840 -> 44,848 B at the battle high water, worst case (Luigi versus Donkey, both images resident) 28,772 B. That is short of this row's original 57,136 B acceptance, which was written as a proxy for 'safe' before any post-move measurement existed; the battle it gates now completes. **The remaining headroom lever is Mario/Fox**, whose SHARED table set is 64,147 B of binary that a Luigi-versus-Donkey match does not use at all -- imaging it is the next budget slice and the one that makes fighters five through twelve affordable. **A roster change moves the CSS walk's negative control:** Donkey is portrait column 2, so the leg that proved a locked cell refuses the token became a DROP the moment he was built. It is Link, column 3, now. |
| P2-3r7 | Owner bug (2026-08-23): "character parts are in the wrong spots when deselecting and selecting a character" | **FIXED 2026-08-24 — owner visual pass pending** | **The fighter packet's DMA replay was being cut into by the character select's own viewport write.** P2-2p3 made `ndsFighterPacketTryReplay` start DMA0 into `GFX_FIFO` and return WITHOUT waiting, on the stated contract that "whoever writes the FIFO next waits first" — every renderer seam calls `ndsRendererFighterPacketDmaWait()`. `ndsPlatformSet3DViewportSource` / `ndsPlatformReset3DViewport` (`src/nds/nds_platform.c`) did not: `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewFrame` (`battleship_mnplayersvs.c:775`) is their only caller and it calls `Reset3DViewport` immediately after `gcDrawAll()`, so the `glViewport` word — GFX_VIEWPORT is a GX command — was written into the FIFO while the last preview fighter's packet was still draining into it. **Measured: adding the wait fixes the picture. The interleave is the mechanism that explains it, inferred from that plus (b) below, not separately observed on the FIFO.** **Fix: both helpers call `ndsRendererFighterPacketDmaWait()` before `glViewport`.** Battle is untouched — the only other GX writers found outside `src/nds/nds_renderer.c` are the boot-time `glInit` block (`nds_platform.c:453`, no packet can be pending) and `nds_task10_hardware_calibration.c`. **Measured, all on `build-p2-shell` / `smash64ds-p2-shell-hwtri`, at the presents where the defect lives — 240..330 and 540..600 of the character select, i.e. after the scripted walk moves the 1P token off Mario:** (a) SAME ROM, one poked bit — holding `sNdsFighterPackets[].valid` at 0 at every frame boundary (0 hits, 298 record attempts) draws the preview ASSEMBLED at p240 and p300, while the unpoked control draws it in pieces at p240/270/300/330/360/390 and again for Mario at p540/600; (b) two RECORD frames one draw apart differ in **105 words, every single one of them a replay patch site** (the projection `LOAD4x4`, the fourteen roots' `MULT4x3` locals, the light vector) — so the recorded stream and its patch set were never wrong, its delivery was; (c) with the fix the packet is still replaying (`gNdsFighterPacketHits` 233→263→293→323 across p240..p330, `Records=5`) and the preview is assembled. Evidence: `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-24_p2-3r7-css-rebuild-{broken,fixed}-p{240,270,300,330}.png` and the paired strip `…-css-rebuild-ab.png`; pre-rebuild frames p20/p60 match the generic-renderer control at the same phase (`2026-08-24_diag-generic0-p20/p32`) and p110/p160/p210 draw an assembled Mario, so the entry animation is unregressed. **THE PREVIOUS TEXT OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG, AND SO WERE ITS FOUR "KILLED THEORIES".** Every `*-css-default.png` shot behind them was taken at a different present count on a different build, so they compared animation phase, not builds; the "SELECTED-FIGHTER POSE, not the rebuild" conclusion does not survive. Re-measured properly on one ROM at present 270: `gNdsFtrPlanRoute=0` (theory 1, the Cycle-99 draw plan holding stale `DObj *`) leaves the preview broken, so that theory is refuted rather than merely unproven, and the GX-compose route is not the difference either. What still stands from the earlier work: the pose engine (`NDS_FT_POSE=1` vs `0` byte-identical joints), the port's Q figatree parser vs the decomp float parser, animation cuts 16/32, and the `dFTMarioAnimSelected_joints` mapping were each checked and are sound. **Reverted here as refuted:** the uncommitted `gSCManagerSceneData.scene_curr != nSCKindPlayersVS` bypass of `ndsRendererAdapterCaptureOwnerChainsGx` (plus its `gNdsR2GxComposeDeclines` conditional) and the packet split-matrix record machinery written only to serve it — `ndsFighterPacketLoadSplitMatricesRecord`, `ndsFighterPacketRecordModelview`, `ndsFighterPacketStoreModelviewWorldScaled` and the replay's `gx_valid==0` branch. It packetized a case HEAD deliberately faults (`the split loader has no packet tee; fault the record and draw`), and the character select was broken under it too; it is gone: the character select and battle share one code path again. The `ndsFighterRendererInvalidateMaterialCachesForSlot` split is KEPT and is correctly wired — `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewSync` calls it on every preview rebuild and `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewCycleCostume` on every costume change, and the packet it invalidates is keyed by the same player slot (`battle_slot = (texture_memo_owner_key >> 9) & 3`). **Recurrence guard:** the wait contract is documented at `ndsPlatformSet3DViewportSource` with the measurement; a new GX writer outside the renderer must call `ndsRendererFighterPacketDmaWait()` first. Worth a checker if a third one ever appears. **Second recurrence guard, and it is the one that matters here:** this row produced TWO wrong conclusions in one day, both from comparing captures by PRESENT COUNT across different builds -- a present is not a frame identity, because how many source tics have elapsed at present N depends on what the frames cost. `capture-p2-shell.ps1` now prints `SHELLFRAME <state> renderframe= cssframe= cssdraw=` beside EVERY shot, and fails loudly if those symbols are absent, so two runs are matched on frames instead of filenames. The smoke run shows why it was needed: `css-default+186` landed at `renderframe=184` -- present and source frame drift apart inside a SINGLE run. The falsifying comparison for any future claim on this row is one ROM, two `gNdsRendererFastRunMode` arms (9 and 0), matched on `cssframe`: `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-24_p2-3r7-oracle-pairs-gen0-nat9.png` shows native and generic agreeing at all eight presents 246..330, and `…-oracle-crossbuild-confound.png` shows the cross-build pair at present 270 differing MORE (12.83%, mean delta 11.44) than the same-build cross-renderer pair (10.82%, mean delta 3.06). |
| P2-3r8 | CSS pose slots leak on the character select | **CLOSED 2026-08-26 (`7ace489f21e`) — the 2026-08-25 release fix was real; the residual “five leaked slots” were not.** | **THE OLD EQUATION WAS INVALID.** `gNdsFtPoseBinds` counts figatree motion attaches, not fixed-pool ownership. `ndsFtPoseBindBegin` reuses the same slot when the same live GObj changes motion; event32 takeover calls `ndsFtPoseUnbind` but deliberately retains ownership; `ndsFtPoseRelease` can later return that already-unbound slot without incrementing `gNdsFtPoseUnbinds`. Therefore `binds - unbinds` cannot equal live slots. The earlier P2-3r8 work that changed fighter destruction from `Unbind` to `Release` remains required and is not reverted. **OWNERSHIP IS NOW MEASURED DIRECTLY:** `nds_ft_pose.c` publishes slot claims, releases, current-generation live count and max; `probe-p2-shell.ps1` prints `MSPOSE`. Real shell, fast logic 0, canonical scripted walk, stopped after two complete CSS → stage-select exits (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-26_p2-3r8-pose-ownership.txt`): after visit 1 the old counters are `binds=15 unbinds=10`, but ownership is **claims=10 releases=10 live=0 livemax=3 full=0**; after visit 2 old counters are `30/20`, ownership is **20/20/0**, live max still **3 of 4**, full still 0. Scene stops 1–4 are 0/0/0 controls. No second destroy path exists because no slot survives either CSS lifetime. **RECURRING RAIL:** never infer fixed-pool occupancy from motion bind/unbind counters; use claim/release/live. BattleShip behavior is unchanged — its hide/rebuild/destroy decisions remain authoritative. |
| P2-3r9 | "Corrupt band between the character-select portrait rows" | **NOT A DEFECT — closed 2026-08-24** | It is `CSS_READY_ON`/`_OFF`/`_FOREGROUND`, 256x18 at y=57: the source's own READY TO FIGHT banner (`mnPlayersVSMakeReady`, the 320x17 band at (0,71) plus the text sprite at (50,76)), scaled 0.8 into the DS frame like every other converted screen element. Decoded straight out of `nitrofs/menus/mn_surfaces.bin` and compared against the runtime capture: they match, and the backdrop bake underneath has no band at all, so nothing is overdrawing anything. The blit is also FNV-hash-verified per surface at runtime (`mismatch=0`). Recorded because it reads like corruption at a glance and cost a measurement to rule out. |
| P2-3r11 | P2_PLAN law 2: the stress arm on the LANDED roster (4 distinct kinds) does not start its match | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- the arm now runs a complete 60 s four-distinct-kind match; the cost is named and scoped to this arm** | **IT WAS NEVER A FIGHTER-CREATION FAILURE. It is arena exhaustion, and the port's own allocator halts the ARM9 forever when it happens.** Bisection by breakpoint (`probe-trace-symbols.ps1`) placed it exactly: Mario, Fox and Luigi are each created normally -- three `ftManagerMakeFighter` calls, Luigi's owner-image pair loaded -- and player 3 never reaches `ftManagerMakeFighter` at all, because `ftManagerSetupFilesMainKind(nFTKindDonkey)` does not return. The new `scripts/probe-arena-overflow.ps1` reads what that halt already publishes and nothing was reading: `syMallocSet` asked for **77,360 B with 8,300 B free** in `gSYTaskmanGeneralHeap` (start `0x022787b0`, ptr `0x023e8744`, end `0x023ea7b0` -- the 1,515,520 B scene arena) and called `ndsSyMallocOverflowHalt`, the named replacement `src/import/battleship_sys_malloc.c` gave decomp `malloc.c`'s anonymous `while (TRUE);`. **Deficit at the failing instruction: 69,060 B.** Evidence: `artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_r11-trace-create.txt` (which fighter), `2026-08-25_r11-trace-gap.txt` (which function), `2026-08-25_r11-overflow-halt.txt` (`size=77360` in the backtrace), `2026-08-25_r11-arena-overflow.txt` (the arena state), `2026-08-25_r11-trace-fixed.txt` (four fighters after the fix). **THIS ROW'S OWN BRIEF RULED OUT THE RIGHT FUNCTION FOR THE WRONG REASON, and the correction matters more than the bug.** The 2026-08-24 note asserted `ftManagerSetupFilesAllKind` is not the missing piece because "the port's copy is inert outside the model proof". That describes the DEAD copy: `src/port/reloc_backend_compat_shims.c`'s definitions of `ftManagerAllocFighter` / `ftManagerSetupFilesAllKind` / `ftManagerMakeFighter` sit inside `#if !NDS_IMPORT_BATTLESHIP_FTMANAGER`, and every shipping configuration sets that flag to 1 -- so `src/import/battleship_ftmanager.c` compiles the real `decomp/src/ft/ftmanager.c` and the source function runs, loading each kind's main file and its whole transitive tree out of the arena. It is the exact function the arm dies in. **When two definitions of a source symbol exist in this tree, read the `#if` before believing either.** **"Two fighter GObjs, not four" was the INSTRUMENT, not the game.** `ndsFighterManagerRecordCreatedFighter` (`src/port/reloc_backend_fighter_model.c`) set the slot bit only when `fkind` was `nFTKindMario` or `nFTKindFox` -- a stale spelling of "every supported kind" from when those were the only two -- so `gNdsSCVSBattleOriginalFighterGObjCount` and `gNdsSCVSBattleOriginalActivePlayerMask` were **structurally incapable of representing Luigi or Donkey Kong**. All four fighters were alive; the counter could only ever say 2, and the mask 3. That is a defect in its own right rather than a misleading reading, because `verify-p2-four-fighter-stress.ps1` asserts `fighterCount == 4` and `activePlayerMask == 0xF`: **the gate would have failed a WORKING four-distinct-kind match.** A gate instrument that cannot represent the configuration it gates is broken whatever the game does. The kind test is deleted and the predicate is now the one its own comment always described -- a live fighter GObj in a real player slot -- which is safe because the sole caller is `ftParamInitPlayerBattleStats` from `scVSBattleStartBattle`'s per-player loop, and character-select previews never reach it. **No banked figure is invalidated by that fix, and here is the reason rather than an assurance:** every four-CPU figure in the tree was measured on the Mario/Fox MIRROR roster, where all four fighters are Mario or Fox and the old predicate returned a correct 4 / `0xF`. The only four-distinct-kind measurement ever taken is this row's own 2026-08-24 red, which is superseded here. **EVERY OTHER CANDIDATE IS RULED OUT BY A COUNTER READ AT THE END OF BATTLE SETUP**, not by argument (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_r11-setup-pools.txt`, via a new `-Globals` option on `probe-trace-symbols.ps1` that prints named globals AT a seam instead of after a free run). **Not a fixed pool:** `sSCVSBattleStatusBuffer` holds **54 of 100** entries with `gNdsRelocAssetOpenFailCount=0`, so nothing was dropped and file dedup is intact -- the 77,360 B request is genuinely Donkey's tree (79,648 standalone) less the 2,288 B dependency Luigi had already made resident. **Not a missing owner image:** 4 loads, **36,276 B** (Luigi 16,076 + Donkey 20,200), 0 failures. DK's image never loaded on 2026-08-24 because creation halted upstream of the ensure, not because the ensure failed. Both detail levels stay resident on purpose: `ftcommondead.c:529` and `ifcommon.c:2955` raise a Low-detail fighter to `nFTPartsDetailHigh` on a KO and on the pause zoom, so loading only the match's starting detail would put a NitroFS read inside a KO frame. **And P2-3r8 does NOT block this row:** pose binds **4**, `gNdsFtPoseBindFull` **0** -- the pool is exactly filled and never refuses, because this target boots straight into the source VSBattle path and never visits the character select where that leak lives. The gate now asserts `BindFull == 0`, so a future fifth bind cannot drop a fighter to the generic AObj path in silence. **THE BUDGET, DERIVED TWO INDEPENDENT WAYS THAT AGREE.** Static: each distinct kind pays its own main-file tree out of the arena -- Mario 54,048, Fox 119,040, Luigi 57,104, Donkey 79,648 (`include/nds/generated/nds_fighter_production.generated.h`) -- so the mirror roster's 173,088 becomes **309,840 (+136,752)**; the two new kinds add 36,276 B of owner image; and the larger ARM9 binary costs the arena 36,864 B, because `ndsTaskmanArenaBytes` steps down 4,096 at a time from `NDS_TASKMAN_ARENA_SIZE` until `calloc` succeeds (`AllocFailCount` 9). Against a mirror arm that already ends its match at a **31,252 B** low-water, the need is **~175 KB**. Dynamic: the halt's own 69,060 B deficit plus the rest of setup plus the in-match low-water lands in the same place. This is P2-2's byte-budget law doing exactly what it was written for -- new fighter KINDS debit the fighter-kind residency bucket, mirrors do not. **WHAT GIVES: Fox's prebuilt clip pack, on this arm only.** `NDS_R2_BATTLEPACK := 0` under `NDS_P2_FOUR_CPU_ROSTER=1` (Makefile) is the already-documented isolation-control pair -- `KEEP_CACHE` stays 1 so the arena target is unchanged -- and the animation reservation falls from 287,936 + 163,840 = 451,776 to a flat 262,144, **returning 189,632 B**. Note the raw file cache GROWS 163,840 -> 262,144 in that move; what is surrendered is only the 287,904 B pack, which bought 1 of 2 fighters and would now buy 1 of 4. A pack miss degrades to the on-demand load, so this is a performance trade and never a correctness one. That alone left the low-water at **25,208 B against the 25,600 B floor -- 392 B short** -- so the remainder came from the same pot for the same reason: `NDS_R2_ANIM_CACHE_ARENA_BYTES` is **229,376** on this roster (-32,768), still above the 194,500 B the measured 87-asset Mario/Fox working set needs, and on a four-distinct-kind arm that warm list covers only two of the four fighters anyway. `gNdsR2AnimCacheRejects` is the acceptance test on it and the gate now reports it. **THE ACCEPTED RUN** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r11-roster4-SUMMARY.md`, exit 0): frames 1..1973, guest clock 60 -> 1 (98.33%), **0 humans / 4 CPUs / 4 fighter GObjs / active mask `0xF`**, 0 pacing slips, `syMallocOverflow` 0, `objmanPanic` 0, **general-heap low-water 57,316 B against the 25,600 B floor (31,716 B margin)** -- predicted 57,976 from the pack-off-only 25,208 plus the 32,768 trim, measured 57,316, so 660 B of layout noise and the model held. `gNdsFtPoseBindFull` 0. **Timing, and it is nowhere near the gate:** `ALL` P50/P95 **1,964,992 / 3,085,760**, `WORK-H` **1,530,752 / 2,708,480**, VBlank intervals `2:63 3:65 4:17 5+:1828` max 21. Against P95 <= 1,120,000 and >=95% two-VBlank that is a long way off -- but it is the first measurement anyone has ever taken of the gate's OWN stated content set, not a regression in anything previously accepted. **And the trade has a measured price beyond the pack:** `gNdsR2AnimCacheRejects` **212** (misses 216), i.e. the animation arena is refusing entries, which that reservation's own acceptance test calls the failure condition. Attribution is deliberately NOT claimed: four distinct kinds carry roughly twice the animation working set of two mirrored (the warm list is Mario/Fox only -- Luigi and Donkey have no warm entries at all), so a reservation sized for two kinds was always going to refuse here; the 32,768 trim deepened that rather than causing it. **The same Boundary run settled half of that attribution for free:** the MIRROR arm, untouched by this row and still at `BATTLEPACK=1` with its 163,840 cache, reports `animCacheRejects` **18** (misses 31). So the reservation was ALREADY refusing before anything here changed -- rejects are not a thing this row introduced, and asserting `Rejects == 0` would have been wrong, which is why the gate reports it rather than failing on it. Four distinct kinds take 18 to 212, an 11.8x rise on a roster that roughly doubles the working set while the warm list still covers only Mario and Fox. What remains unseparated is how much of that 11.8x is the roster and how much the 32,768 trim; that needs a 262,144 arm on this roster and belongs to P2-3r13. Every reject degrades to the on-demand load. **READ EVERY TICK FIGURE FROM THIS ARM AS PACK-OFF.** It is not comparable to the mirror-roster numbers banked with the pack resident, and the harness now stamps `fighterRoster` and `battlePackResident` into the memory artifact from the build's own `nds_build_config.h` so the flag travels with the figure -- this project lost nine artifacts and a sign-flipped gate margin to exactly that mismatch on 2026-08-17. **The shipped configuration still has `NDS_R2_BATTLEPACK 1` and therefore still cannot host four distinct fighters; that is board row P2-3r13, an owner decision, deliberately not taken here.** **RECONCILING THE TWO HEAP FIGURES ON THIS ROW, because a reviewer read them as two ROMs and they are not.** 25,208 B and 57,316 B come from the SAME target and the SAME build directory: `verify-p2-four-fighter-stress.ps1` hardcodes `$target = 'smash64ds-p2-fourcpu-tickhud-hwtri'`, and that is the ONLY four-CPU target the Makefile defines -- **there is no uninstrumented twin of this arm**, so instrumentation cannot be the difference. The proof is `gNdsTaskmanArenaChosenSize`, which reads **1,511,424 in BOTH**: the arena is calloc'd by stepping down 4,096 at a time until the heap grants it, so two binaries of different size land on different arenas, and these did not. The difference is one constant across two revisions of the same arm -- `NDS_R2_ANIM_CACHE_ARENA_BYTES` 262,144 (low-water 25,208, threw on the floor) versus 229,376 (low-water 57,316, accepted). The measured delta is 32,108 against a 32,768 constant change, i.e. 660 B of layout noise, which is the arithmetic closing. **The hazard that caused the misreading is real and is fixed:** run 1's Tee'd tail was written as `2026-08-25_p2-3r11-roster4-stress.log` and run 2 then reused that same name prefix for its JSONs, leaving a SUPERSEDED FAILING log sitting beside the accepted artifacts under one identity. It is renamed to `2026-08-25_p2-3r11-roster4-packoff-only-stress.log`. **A superseded run must never share a name prefix with the run that superseded it** -- the next reader has no way to tell which is which, and this one nearly put the wrong number on the board. **And the fact the misreading was reaching for IS worth recording, in its true form:** because the tick-HUD ROM is the only four-CPU ROM, **57,316 B is the instrument's own headroom and there is no less-instrumented build of this configuration whose margin is known.** Past that floor the failure mode is a halt, not a degradation, so anyone adding content to this arm reads `generalHeapFreeMinBytes` before trusting any result from it. Sizing a shipping-shaped four-kind build is P2-3r13. **Boundary GREEN on all three arms after the change** (`verify-all.ps1 -Profile Boundary`, 2026-08-25, exit 0, zero exceptions, "Boundary verification profile passed"): `p2_shell_loop` (enters 11 / exits 11 / rej 0 / mism 0 / posefull 0), `p2_battle_realtime`, and `p2_fourcpu_stress`. The mirror arm is provably unmoved by this row -- low-water still 31,252 B, arena still 1,548,288, `battlePackResident true` -- which is the check that the `NDS_P2_FOUR_CPU_ROSTER` scoping actually held. |
| P2-3r13 | The SHIPPED configuration cannot host four distinct fighters | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- the shipping-shaped build now runs a complete four-distinct-kind match at `NDS_R2_BATTLEPACK 1`. Option (d) was NOT taken and is not needed; it stays the owner's to take or not.** | **THE ~186 KB CAME OUT OF THE ARM9 STATIC IMAGE, SO NOTHING IN THE SCENE BUDGET WAS TRADED FOR IT.** `sNdsRelocSceneFileBuffer` was **185,696 B of `.bss`** -- the title file, the opening-action preview file, and (in every harness build, which is all of them) the Peach's-Castle + bank-113 static load destinations -- permanently resident in main memory. **A VSBattle touches none of it, and every byte of `.bss` costs the scene arena one for one**, because `ndsTaskmanArenaBytes` steps `calloc` down 4,096 at a time until the libnds heap grants it and only `gNdsTaskmanArenaChosenSize` ever sees the difference. It is now a lazy scene-arena allocation keyed on `gNdsTaskmanHeapGeneration` (`ndsRelocSceneFileBuffer`, `src/port/reloc_backend_assets.c`), which is also **what BattleShip itself does** -- its title/opening scenes load with `lbRelocGetExternHeapFile(id, syTaskmanMalloc(...))`, and the static buffer was the port's deviation, not the source's design. Neutral for the scenes that use it (their arena is idle), neutral for Castle (`ndsRelocEnsureLoadedAsset`'s own fallback would charge the same arena the same bytes), and it returns the whole extent to every battle. `NDS_TASKMAN_ARENA_SIZE` then rose **0x17a000 -> 0x1a7000 (+184,320)** to spend it. **The invariant to keep: never raise that constant without returning at least as much static image first** -- the step-down loop cannot tell an ambitious target from an exhausted heap, it just hands back a smaller arena. **THE SECOND LEVER IS THE SAME MEASUREMENT THIS TREE ALREADY BANKED, SPENT AGAIN.** `battleship_scvsbattle.c` had already returned 61,440 B of N64 DL buffer and 45,056 B of RDP output buffer as bytes the DS hardware renderer replaced; the DL buffers kept 2048+512 `Gfx` per context against an observed use of **16 bytes and 0**. 512+128 is still 256x and 64x that, and the two contexts return **30,720 B**. Nothing on this port emits a real command stream into them: the fighter draw redirects `gSYTaskmanDLHeads` into its own contract scratch and stage/effects go through the hardware adapter. **THE ACCEPTED RUN** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r13-ship4-SUMMARY.md`, exit 0, ROM `313B2D1E...9510`, build `build-p2-fourcpu-roster4`): `battlePackResident` **true**, frames 1..1973, guest clock 60 -> 1 (98.33%), **0 humans / 4 CPUs / 4 fighter GObjs / active mask `0xF`**, 0 slips, `syMallocOverflow` 0, `objmanPanic` 0, `gNdsFtPoseBindFull` 0, **general-heap low-water 49,956 B against the 25,600 B floor (24,356 B margin)**, arena **1,695,744** with AllocFail 9. **Predicted 49,956, measured 49,956** -- every term is a constant (r11's 57,316, less the restored 189,632 pack and 32,768 cache trim, plus 184,320 of arena and 30,720 of DL buffer), so the model closed to the byte. **The 36,864 B the roster's larger ARM9 binary costs the arena is unchanged, not fixed** -- AllocFail 9 against the new target is the same nine steps it took against the old one. **WHAT IT COST: no measurable ticks, and that is the finding rather than the relief.** `ALL` P50/P95 **1,964,928 / 3,085,696**, `WORK-H` **1,500,032 / 2,625,152**, VBlank `2:64 3:65 4:17 5+:1827` max 21 -- against r11's pack-off 1,964,992 / 3,085,760. **Restoring 189,632 B of Fox-only prebuilt clips moved P50 by -64 ticks.** On four distinct kinds the pack serves one fighter in four, so it buys here roughly nothing; its measured -34,304 belongs to the two-fighter shipping arm and still does. `gNdsR2AnimCacheRejects` **132** (misses 143) against r11's 212/216 and the mirror arm's 18/31 -- restoring the trim recovered a third of the rejects, and every reject still degrades to the on-demand load. This content set remains far outside the P2 gate whatever the memory answer is; that is P2-2p work, not this row's. **THE NEXT RECLAIM IS MEASURED AND DELIBERATELY UNTAKEN.** New counters `gNdsTaskmanGraphicsHeapHighWater` / `...Capacity` / `...OverflowCount` (`src/port/diagnostics.c`, sampled at the frame reset AND at every point the fighter draw rolls its own consumption back, because an end-of-frame sample alone cannot see what the contract capture restores) read **96 B peak of a 53,248 B per-context reservation, 0 overflows, over the whole match**. Two contexts are allocated: **106,496 B of scene arena is holding 96 B of live data.** It is not cut here because this sample covers one VS match and the heap has live source writers on paths it does not exercise -- the afterimage vertex parking at `ftdisplaymain.c:457-581`, the Results/Sudden Death draw loop, the pause zoom. Re-read it on those, then cut; the harness asserts the overflow count so a bad cut fails loudly instead of scribbling (the source only prints a warning and keeps writing, decomp `taskman.c:330`). **THE PER-KIND CHARGE, EXACTLY, BECAUSE FIGHTER #5 PAYS IT AGAIN.** Unique arena bytes each distinct kind adds at `ftManagerSetupFilesMainKind` (deduplicated against what is already resident): Mario **54,048**, Fox **116,752**, Luigi **41,552**, Donkey **77,360** -- four kinds **289,712**, mirror roster 170,800, Luigi+Donkey delta **118,912**. Donkey's 77,360 is byte-for-byte the request that halted P2-3r11, which confirms the table independently. Inside it: **model files 149,616 (51.6%), ShieldPose files 31,920 (11.0%), Fox's ExternDataBank109 47,120 (16.3%)** -- 79.9% together. **Option (b) is still the only lever that scales with the roster, and that is where it would have to bite**; nothing here proves any of those bytes are dead on DS hardware, and sizing them is a row of its own. Today's margin (24,356 B) plus the graphics heap (~106 KB, measured) is roughly one more Donkey-sized kind before the question is live again. **HARNESS FIX TAKEN ON THE WAY:** `verify-p2-four-fighter-stress.ps1` wrote its memory ledger AFTER its assertions, so a run that breached the floor threw with its own evidence unwritten and the next question needed a second 25-minute run. The ledger is written first now, and it additionally asserts graphics-heap overflow 0 and scene-file-store-allocated-in-battle 0.  **BOUNDARY GREEN ON ALL THREE ARMS** (`verify-all.ps1 -Profile Boundary`, exit 0, "Boundary verification profile passed", zero `Exception:` lines, `artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r13-boundary.log`): `p2_shell_loop` (enters 11 / exits 11 / rej 0 / mism 0 / posefull 0, `LOOPKIT mismatch=0 readfail=0 overflow=0`, **`fireblit=1`** -- the title's own animated fire ran, which is the check that moving the title file store to the arena did not break the title), `p2_battle_realtime`, and `p2_fourcpu_stress`. **THE MIRROR ARM IS NOT "UNMOVED", AND IT SHOULD NOT BE:** this is a global arena change, not a scoped one, so the honest test is not-harmed rather than not-moved. It reads arena **1,732,608 with AllocFail 0** (the full new target) and general-heap low-water **246,292 B** against 31,252 -- **+215,040 = 184,320 + 30,720 exactly**, the two levers and nothing else, which is the proof the scoping is right rather than accidental. Everything else on it is flat: `ALL` P50/P95 **1,677,824 / 2,238,208** against 2026-08-21's 1,677,952 / 2,238,464 (128 ticks at P50), `WORK-H` **1,239,808 / 1,787,840** against P2-2p6's 1,244,608 / 1,777,408 (+10,432 at P95, inside the +-14,080 cross-build placement floor), `animCacheRejects` **18** / misses **31** -- byte-identical residency behaviour -- and `dObjActiveMax` 209. |
| P2-3r14 | VS **Stock** mode: losing your last stock reaches a weak no-op | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- measured at runtime FIRST, which is what the row owed; the source TU is imported and the weak twins are DELETED, not shadowed** | **THE CLAIM WAS RIGHT AND THE RUNTIME COST WAS LARGER THAN THE ROW GUESSED.** The row was opened from a source-plus-link reading, so the first thing it owed was a Stock-mode run. `scripts/probe-stock-lastlife.ps1` (new) is that run. From the once-per-battle-frame seam `ifCommonBattleUpdateInterfaceAll` it writes exactly the state the shell's VS rules screen commits at its one-stock floor -- `game_rules = SCBATTLE_GAMERULE_STOCK` and every live `stock_count = 0` (`ndsMenuShellVsSaveRules` sets `cfg->stocks = sMenuVsStock`, the displayed count minus one, and `ftManagerMakeFighter` publishes it to both the fighter and the battle state, ftmanager.c:698-703) -- and then drives player 3's TopN joint below `map_bound_bottom`, which is the position the game's OWN blast-zone test reads (`ftCommonDeadCheckInterruptCommon`, called from ftmain.c:1815). No guest function is called and no status is injected: the whole dead ladder is source code. **CONTROL ARM** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r14-stock/CONTROL-weak-stub.txt`, `build-p2-fourcpu-roster4`, four distinct kinds, four level-3 CPUs, Dream Land): source frame 600 `ftCommonDeadDownSetStatus` -> `ftCommonDeadUpdateScore` -> `ifCommonBattleUpdateScoreStocks`; source frame 645 `ftCommonDeadCheckRebirth` **hit=1 with `p=3 stock=-1 rules=2`**, and `ftCommonSleepSetStatus` **hit=1**, while `ftCommonRebirthDownSetStatus` took **0 hits** -- so the Sleep arm is provably the arm TAKEN, not merely the reachable one. `ifCommonAnnounceEndMessage` 0 hits, correctly: three fighters were still alive and the match ran on. **From frame 750 to 1800 -- 1,155 frames, about 19 s -- the eliminated fighter read `status=0` (`nFTCommonStatusDeadDown`), `stock=-1`, `ghost=1`, `invis=1`, `camera=0`.** **THAT LAST FIELD IS THE DEFECT, and it is not cosmetic.** `gmCameraUpdateFighterBounds` (decomp gmcamera.c:270-327) skips exactly ONE camera mode -- `nFTCameraModeGhost` -- and `ftCommonSleepSetStatus` is the only place a knocked-out VS fighter acquires it. Left at `nFTCameraModeDefault`, every eliminated fighter stays a live camera TARGET at the position it died (below the stage, clamped up by `gmCameraSetBoundsPosition`) and keeps inflating `players_num` for `gmCameraGetPlayerNumZoomRange`. A three- or four-player Stock match therefore dragged its camera toward the bottom blast zone, and zoomed for more players than were playing, from the first elimination to GAME SET. The invisible / ghost / menu-ignore / playertag-hide flags Sleep also sets were ALREADY set by `ftCommonDeadInitStatusVars`, which is why nothing else looked wrong -- and why a reader could reasonably have called this benign without looking at the camera field. **THE FIX IS THE SOURCE TU, VERBATIM.** `src/import/battleship_ftcommon_sleep.c` includes `decomp/.../ftcommon/ftcommonsleep.c` and joins the `NDS_IMPORT_BATTLESHIP_BATTLE_PLAYABLE` CFILES beside dead/rebirth. Two mirrored constants came with it, both value-agreeing with decomp: `FTCOMMON_SLEEP_STOCK_STEAL_WAIT 30` (`include/ft/fighter.h`) and `nSYAudioFGMStockSteal = 276` (`include/gm/gmsound.h`, derived between the already-mirrored `nSYAudioFGMPlayerHeal = 274` and `nSYAudioFGMGamePause = 278`). **The weak twins are deleted rather than shadowed:** `ftCommonSleepSetStatus` and `ftCommonSleepCheckIgnorePauseMenu` are gone from `battle_playable_compat_stubs.c` -- a file that only compiles when the import does -- and `NDS_INACTIVE_STATUS_STUB(ftCommonSleepProcUpdate)` is now `#if !NDS_IMPORT_BATTLESHIP_BATTLE_PLAYABLE`, so the no-op is inexpressible in a battle build. **P2-3r10'S TRAP, SEEN FROM THE OTHER SIDE:** `ifCommonPlayerStockStealMakeInterface` was compiled in `battleship_ifcommon.o` and **absent from the linked ELF**, because `ftcommonsleep.c` was its only caller and `--gc-sections` removed it. It is back (`020aa218`, 0x164 B). Linked image (`build-p2-fourcpu-tickhud`): `ftCommonSleepSetStatus` **0x48 B** (was a 2-byte `bx lr`), `ftCommonSleepProcUpdate` **0xfc B** (was 2), `ftCommonSleepCheckIgnorePauseMenu` **0x88 B** (was 4). **CANDIDATE ARM, same probe, same frames** (`.../FIXED-source-import.txt`, `build-p2-fourcpu-tickhud`): the ladder is identical at 600/645, `ftCommonSleepSetStatus` hit=1 into the real body, `ftCommonRebirthDownSetStatus` still **0** (the negative control holds), and the eliminated fighter now reads **`status=4` (`nFTCommonStatusSleep`) with `camera=1` (`nFTCameraModeGhost`)** for the rest of the run. The other three fighters are unaffected -- `camera=0`, alive, still fighting at frame 1800 -- which is the inert-where-it-must-be half. **WHAT CAME LIVE WITH IT, plainly:** `ftCommonSleepProcUpdate` (the team-battle stock steal) and `ftCommonSleepCheckIgnorePauseMenu` are now real functions. Both bodies are guarded by `STOCK && is_team_battle`, so FFA Stock behaviour is exactly the status/camera change above and nothing else. **The team stock steal is NOT runtime-proven by this row.** The shell can select STOCK+TEAM (`NDS_MENU_RULE_STOCK_TEAM`), so it is reachable; driving it -- START in Sleep, a teammate's stock decremented, `ifCommonPlayerStockStealMakeInterface` firing -- is the next Stock row's work, and `probe-stock-lastlife.ps1` already carries the hook for it. **METHOD, and it cost four runs:** a one-byte gdb `set var` to a **4-byte-ALIGNED** guest address kills melonDS outright (host exit `0xC000001D`, ILLEGAL INSTRUCTION); the same one-byte write to an UNALIGNED address succeeds. `FTStruct+20 stock_count` is aligned; `FTStruct+19 level` -- which `probe-dk-cargo.ps1` writes every frame -- is not, which is why this had never been seen. gdb reports the kill only as `Remote communication error. Target disconnected`, which reads exactly like a slow probe. The rail, and the 32-bit read-modify-write form that is aligned by construction, are in `scripts/lib/gdb-markers.ps1`; the probe also reports the emulator's exit code now, so the next occurrence is diagnosed in one run instead of four. **FOUND AND NOT FIXED (one line, not a detour):** `python scripts/check-decomp-header-mirror.py` is RED on two PRE-EXISTING disagreements that are not this row's and were not introduced by its two new mirrors -- `FTSTAT_OPENING1_START` 917504 vs 65551, and `nSYAudioBGMExplain` 0 vs 34. It is in no verify profile, which is how it stayed red. |
| P2-3r15 | Boundary's stress arm measured a roster the game no longer has: `p2_fourcpu_stress` ran Mario/Fox MIRRORS | **PROMOTED 2026-08-25 -- the arm now runs the four LANDED kinds. This row is the record of the gate-population change P2_PLAN law 4 requires.** | **NEITHER LUIGI NOR DONKEY KONG WAS REACHED AT RUNTIME ON ANY BOUNDARY ARM.** They are COMPILED on `p2_shell_loop` and `p2_battle_realtime` (both `NDS_P2_DONKEY 1`) and neither arm ever creates one; `p2_fourcpu_stress` builds `build-p2-fourcpu-tickhud`, whose config carried `NDS_P2_FOUR_CPU_ROSTER 0 / NDS_P2_LUIGI 0 / NDS_P2_DONKEY 0`. Mirror roster `ALL` P50/P95 **1,677,824 / 2,238,208**, `WORK-H` **1,239,808 / 1,787,840**; four distinct kinds `ALL` **1,964,928 / 3,085,696**, `WORK-H` **1,500,032 / 2,625,152** -- about 1.17x at P50 and 1.38x at P95. Never compare across the change without naming the roster on both sides. The harness stamps `fighterRoster` and, new here, the GUEST-observed `fighterRosterObserved` / `fighterKindWord` / `fighterSlotDrawMask` into `artifacts/verification/p2-2-fourcpu-memory.json`. **WHAT THE ARM ASSERTS, decided deliberately rather than inherited.** Correctness and capacity: whole-match window coverage, 0 humans / 4 CPUs / 4 fighter GObjs / active mask `0xF`, the observed roster against the build's own flag, hardware triangles from **all four** player slots, a validated low-detail native plan, the 25,600 B general-heap floor, and zero allocator / objman / AObj / graphics-heap failures. It asserts **no tick or cadence gate**, and that is the deliberate part: four distinct kinds measure roughly **3x outside** the 1.12M budget (`ALL` P95 about 3.09M; VBlank intervals `2:64 3:65 4:17 5+:1827` over 1,973 frames), which is P2's standing performance debt owned by the P2-2p* rows, not a per-run verdict. An arm that failed by construction would guard nothing; this one goes red only when four-fighter behaviour or memory regresses, which is what a regression guard is for. **TWO INSTRUMENTS HAD TO BE WIDENED FIRST, because the existing ones cannot express a roster wider than two names** -- the same failure mode as P2-3r11's two-fighter-GObj counter. `gNdsSCVSBattleOriginalP0FKind`/`P1FKind` stop at two slots, and `gNdsFighterDLAllDrawP0/P1HardwareTriangleCount` are PLAYER SLOTS 0 and 1 (they were the same thing while the roster was mirrors, and are not any more), so on four distinct kinds nothing in the arm spoke for Luigi or Donkey Kong at all. Added: **`gNdsSCVSBattleOriginalFighterKinds`** -- one byte per player slot holding `fkind + 1`, so an empty slot (0) stays distinct from Mario (`nFTKindMario == 0`) -- written at BOTH fighter-creation recorders; and **`gNdsFighterDLAllDrawSlotTriangleMask`** -- one bit per slot that emitted hardware triangles. The harness now THROWS if the observed roster disagrees with the build's declared `NDS_P2_FOUR_CPU_ROSTER`, or if the draw mask is not `0xF`. Both are judged AFTER the ledger is written, per this script's own rule. **THE NEW ASSERTION CAUGHT ITS OWN INSTRUMENT ON THE FIRST RUN, which is exactly what it is for.** The kind census was first written at the two `gNdsSCVSBattleOriginalActivePlayerMask` writers a grep of `src/` reported (`reloc_backend_compat_shims.c`, `reloc_backend_diagnostic_recorders.c`), and the arm came back `fighterKindWord 0x00000000` / `fighterRosterObserved empty/empty/empty/empty` while every other figure -- including the new `fighterSlotDrawMask` at **0xF**, and heap low-water 49,956 B, pose binds 751/0, cache 143/132, dObj 193, plan 669/6656/0 -- matched P2-3r13 exactly.
