The stress arm's own ledger, read out of the GUEST: `fighterRosterObserved` **Mario/Fox/Luigi/Donkey**, `fighterKindWord` **0x03050201**, `fighterSlotDrawMask` **0xF** (all four slots drew), frames 1..1973 / clock 60 -> 1 (98.33%), 0 humans / 4 CPUs / 4 fighter GObjs / mask `0xF`, `battlePackResident` true, general-heap low-water **49,956 B** against the 25,600 floor, arena 1,695,744 (AllocFail 9), pose binds 751 / bindFull 0, anim cache 143/132, `dObjActiveMax` 193, `syMallocOverflow` / `objmanPanic` 0 / 0, plan build/hit/mismatch 669 / 6,656 / 0. Timing, reported and NOT gated: `ALL` P50/P95 **1,964,992 / 3,085,696**, `WORK-H` **1,493,888 / 2,616,384**, VBlank intervals `2:66 3:62 4:18 5+:1827`, max 21, slips 0 -- inside noise of P2-3r13's accepted lab run of the same roster (1,964,928 / 3,085,696), which is the check that the promotion measures the population r13 accepted rather than a new one. ROM SHA-256 `0A7B02C8...704D`. **ONE SIDE EFFECT, and it is its own row:** the first Boundary run of this change turned `p2_battle_realtime` red on a BGM ADPCM guard. That is board row **P2-3r16** -- a pre-existing unbracketed load burst on the character select's second entry, which this change's code placement exposed and which is now fixed at its seam. |
| P2-3r16 | The character select's SECOND entry kills the menu BGM: a whole scene-entry load burst runs unbracketed | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- surfaced by P2-3r15's own Boundary run, attributed in two focused probes, fixed with the bracket P2-3r12 already established** | **HOW IT SURFACED, and why it is not noise.** `p2_battle_realtime`'s BGM ADPCM guard (`verify-battle-mariofox-gcrunall-loop-harness.ps1`) went RED with `gNdsAudioBgmSeamMissCount` **1** and `gNdsAudioBgmErrorStopCount` **1** where it requires 0/0, on the tree carrying P2-3r15's counters -- and GREEN on the same tree an hour earlier without them. It reproduced byte-identically on a `-NoBuild` re-run of the same ROM (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r14-stock/RED-bgm-seam-before-bracket.log`), so it is a deterministic property of that binary, not a flake. **ATTRIBUTION, two `probe-trace-symbols.ps1` runs on `build-p2-shell`.** The first (`.../bgmseam-brackets.txt`) printed the payload/seam counters at every `ndsAudioBgmSuspendForBlockingLoad` / `...Resume...` pair: all fourteen CSS preview-rebuild brackets are clean, and the miss appears BETWEEN suspend 12 (walk step 30, `gNdsRelocAssetPayloadReadCount` 1,194, seammiss 0) and suspend 13 (step 46, 1,675 reads, seammiss 1) -- i.e. outside every bracket. The second (`.../bgmseam-attributed-to-css-entry2.txt`) named the screen: `ndsMenuShellRunCharSelect` hit **2** entered at 1,637 reads / seammiss 0, and `ndsAudioBgmFailPlayback` fired at 1,810 reads / seammiss 1, with `gNdsSCVSBattlePlacementInitCount` 0 throughout -- the run never left the menus. **THE DEFECT.** `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewInit` (`src/import/battleship_mnplayersvs.c`) is a blocking-load burst: `ftManagerSetupFilesAllKind` for every admitted kind -- FOUR on the landed roster -- plus the preview-owner warm, all synchronous NitroFS reads, none of it bracketed. Its own comment asserted *"this function runs before ndsMenuShellCssPlayBgm"*, which is true of the CSS's own track and false of the one still playing when the player backs out of the stage select. `ndsAudioBgmHandleSeam` then correctly calls the overrun an underrun and `ndsAudioBgmFailPlayback` stops the stream, so the music is gone for the rest of the screen. On the shipping ROM the same path exists: back out of the stage select and the menu music dies. **THE FIX is the remedy P2-3r12 already established one function away:** `ndsAudioBgmSuspendForBlockingLoad()` at the top of `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewInit` (after its `sNdsPlayersVSPreviewActive` early-out, before any I/O) and `ndsAudioBgmResumeAfterBlockingLoad()` at its single exit. Suspend returns immediately when nothing is playing, so a FIRST entry is unaffected and the suspend/resume pairing still counts. The stale comment is corrected in place. **CAUSATION, stated honestly: P2-3r15 did not introduce this, it EXPOSED it.** Whether one character-select entry frame overruns one 8,196-byte packet moves with code placement, so a few added instructions anywhere can flip the arm either way -- which is exactly why the burst has to be bracketed rather than left to alignment. A guard that passes because the binary happens to be laid out favourably is not a guard. **STILL OPEN:** the burst itself is real work on the critical path of a menu screen -- four kinds' file trees re-set up on every CSS entry -- and bracketing it makes it silent, not cheap. Making a CSS re-entry genuinely storage-free is the follow-up P2-3r12 already named, and `gNdsRelocAssetPayloadReadCount` is how it will be judged. |
| P2-3r10 | P2_PLAN law 7 sweep + the cargo matrix: what of `ftcommon` and `ftdonkey` this port carries, and does it BEHAVE | **VERIFIED 2026-08-25 -- matrix closed in `docs/p2/fighters/dk.md`; ONE defect found and fixed at its owning seam; two of this row's own 2026-08-24 premises were wrong and are corrected below** | **THE CARGO LADDER IS SOURCE CODE ON THE LIVE PATH, EXCEPT WHERE ONE `#define` STRANDED IT.** `src/import/battleship_donkey.c` includes eleven behavior TUs verbatim -- `ftdonkeyspecialn/hi/lw` plus all **eight** `ftdonkeythrowf*` cargo TUs -- and `ftmain.c:64` pulls `ftdonkeystatus.h` with all 30 statuses. **Premise correction 1: "nine cargo TUs" was a miscount; the directory holds eight.** **Premise correction 2, and it changes what the matrix had to prove: `HeavyThrow{F,B,F4,B4}` are NOT cargo throws.** They are DK's heavy-ITEM throws -- `ftCommonHeavyThrowDecideSetStatus` (`ftcommonitemthrow.c:208`) picks a common `nFTCommonStatusHeavyThrow*` then adds `nFTCommonStatusHeavyThrow4Start` *only for Donkey Kong*, which is why the four sit in his status table, and their only gate is `ftCommonHeavyThrowCheckInterruptCommon`, whose first test is `fp->item_gobj != NULL`. Items are P2-5, so with items off they are unreachable and `battleship_ftcommon_itemthrow.c`'s deliberately-false interrupt is correct. **The fighter-cargo release is ONE status pair** -- `ThrowFF` (ground) / `ThrowAirFF` (air) with the air back-turn variant -- not four. DK carrying a crate reuses the same `ThrowFWait` ladder, which is the connection P2-5 will have to re-check. **VERIFICATION METHOD, because "the source is included" is not a behavior claim.** The port's seams are `#define` redirects, weak stubs and `ndsBase*` renames, any of which can silently displace one hop, so every row was checked on the LINKED IMAGE (`builds/build-p2-fourcpu-roster4/...elf`, the shipping-shaped four-kind build r13 accepted): the ftcommon capture/throw public wrappers are unconditional pass-throughs under `NDS_IMPORT_BATTLESHIP_FTMANAGER`; `ndsBaseFTCommonThrowProcUpdate` disassembles to the fkind test then `bl ftCommonCaptureShoulderedSetStatus` + `bl ftDonkeyThrowFWaitSetStatus`; `ftDonkeyThrowFWaitProcInterrupt` disassembles to the complete five-way ladder in source order, with the Pass and Turn setters inlined and visible as `ftCommonPassSetStatusParam(gobj, 0xF1, 1.0, 0)` and `ftMainSetStatus(gobj, 0xEF, ...)`; `ndsBaseFTCommonCaptureShoulderedProcInterrupt` disassembles to the full mash-out, and its two release descriptors are byte-exact in `.main.rw` (`...KnockbackCatch` = `361,100,30,0`, `...KnockbackCapture` = `361,80,0,20`). **The status table itself had to be checked rather than assumed**, because `include/ft/ftchar/ftdonkey/ftdonkeystatus.h` shadows the decomp header: it is a forwarding shim that supplies a 16-entry `NDS_FT_STATUS_STUB16` when DK is out. `nm -S` settles it -- `dFTDonkeySpecialStatusDescs` is **0x258 = 600 B = 30 entries** of the 20-byte `FTStatusDesc` (Mario's is 0xb4 = 9), i.e. the source table. Per-row evidence is in `docs/p2/fighters/dk.md`. **THE DEFECT: hitting DK while he carries someone put him in an ordinary Damage status while `catch_gobj` still pointed at the shouldered victim.** `battleship_ftcommon_damage.c` redirected the source call `ftDonkeyThrowFDamageSetStatus` to `ndsCompatFTDonkeyThrowFDamageSetStatus`, whose whole body is `ftCommonDamageGotoDamageStatus(fighter_gobj)` under a comment reading *"real DK throw-damage runtime is not imported"* -- true when written, false since `battleship_donkey.c` started compiling `ftdonkeythrowfdamage.c`. **That seam fires on exactly one case and it is this row's case:** `ftCommonDamageUpdateCatchResist` re-applies the zero-knockback/hitlag-stack test that every non-DK arm of `ftCommonDamageCheckCatchResist` implies, so the `ftDonkeyThrowFDamageSetStatus` branch is reachable **only** for a Donkey Kong holding a fighter or a heavy item. Source behaviour is `nFTDonkeyStatusThrowFDamage` (0xF3): a knockback-resisted stagger that KEEPS the cargo, returns to `ThrowFWait`/`ThrowFFall`, and re-syncs the victim through `ftCommonThrownProcPhysics` + `ftParamsUpdateFighterPartsTransformAll`. The port did none of it and left DK in `Damage` -> `Wait` still holding a victim no `Wait` interrupt can throw. **PROVEN TWO INDEPENDENT WAYS BEFORE THE FIX** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r10-dkcargo/PRE-FIX-linked-image.txt`): the disassembly of `ndsBaseFTCommonDamageUpdateCatchResist+0x4a` branches to the compat seam, AND `ftDonkeyThrowFDamageSetStatus` is **defined in `battleship_donkey.o` and absent from the linked ELF** -- compiled, then discarded by `--gc-sections` because the redirect left it callerless. Its sibling `ftDonkeyThrowFDamageProcUpdate`, named by the status table, survives, which is what makes the pair discriminating. **FIXED AT THE OWNING SEAM, AND THE WRONG FORM IS NOW INEXPRESSIBLE:** the redirect, the declaration and the shim's definition are all `#if !NDS_P2_DONKEY`, so with DK in the build the compat name is neither declared nor defined. Post-fix (`.../POST-FIX-linked-image.txt`, build `build-p2-dkcargo`): the call site is `bl 2094f48 <ftDonkeyThrowFDamageSetStatus>`, the restored body loads `r1 = 0xf3` into `ftCommonDamageInitDamageVars` and takes the `catch_gobj` re-sync branch, `ndsCompatFTDonkeyThrowFDamageSetStatus` has 0 occurrences in `nm`, and the stranded-symbol set difference is empty of external-caller entries (the 14 that remain are inlined and each has its in-TU caller listed). **THE GENERAL TRAP, worth more than the bug:** *an imported source function can be compiled and then thrown away because a port `#define` retargeted its only caller.* The detector is a set difference -- symbols defined in `builds/<build>/battleship_*.o` minus symbols in the linked ELF, minus the ones with an in-TU caller. **RUNTIME ENGAGEMENT: THE TWO-FIGHTER CARGO LIFECYCLE RAN END TO END IN A LIVE MATCH, AND IT HAD TO BE DRIVEN RATHER THAN WAITED FOR.** Two whole-match CPU traces prove the SHARED grab seam live but produced no DK cargo pickup between them -- `fourcpu-roster4-trace.txt` (5 grabs, 2 CatchWait, 2 throws) and `dkcargo-bothcpu-trace.txt` (4 grabs, 1 CatchWait, 1 throw) -- because a CPU in CatchWait smashes the stick toward the stage centre (`ftcomputer.c:6026-6035`) and whether that resolves to ThrowF or ThrowB is a coin flip on where the two fighters stand. **Waiting longer was the wrong instrument**, so the new `scripts/probe-dk-cargo.ps1` drives it through the game's own branch: from a once-per-battle-frame hook (`ifCommonBattleUpdateInterfaceAll`) it walks `gGCCommonLinks[3]` and writes exactly two fields -- `fp->level = 9` on every fighter (grab ATTEMPTS, read out of the same field `ftcomputer.c` reads) and `status_vars.common.catchwait.throw_wait = 0` on a Donkey Kong already in CatchWait, which is precisely the condition `ftCommonThrowCheckInterruptCatchWait` reads to choose the forward throw. No guest function is called and no state is injected. **The arm is `smash64ds-battle-playable-proof-hwtri` built as `build-p2-dkcargo` with `NDS_P2_LUIGI=1 NDS_P2_DONKEY=1 NDS_P2_PROOF_FIGHTER0=2 NDS_R2_BOTH_CPU=1` -- Donkey Kong versus Fox, both CPU, Dream Land, one-minute Time, items off, existing flags only, no new target and no new harness mode.** The result (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r10-dkcargo/dkcargo-driven-lvl9-trace.txt`) is the source sequence, in order, at source frame 1483: `CatchWaitSetStatus` -> `CARGOFORCE` -> `ndsBaseFTCommonThrowSetStatus` -> **`ftCommonCaptureShoulderedSetStatus`** (the victim is shouldered) -> **`ftDonkeyThrowFWaitSetStatus`** (DK enters the carry) -> **`ftDonkeyThrowFFSetStatus`** -> `ftDonkeyThrowFFProcUpdate` x39 (the release plays out). Heartbeats at frames 600/1200/1800/2400/3000/3600 show the hook ran the whole match with Donkey cycling normally, which is what makes a zero elsewhere in the ladder readable instead of ambiguous. **WHAT THIS RUN DOES NOT COVER, stated plainly:** the CPU has no `ThrowFWait` handler, so it taps A on its first carry frame and the carry lasted one throw -- `ThrowFWalk`/`ThrowFTurn`/`ThrowFKneeBend`/`ThrowFLanding` and the mash-out never fired, and neither did `ftDonkeyThrowFDamageSetStatus`, because nothing hit DK during that one-frame carry. Those rows rest on source plus the disassembly above (they are all inlined into the `ftDonkeyThrowFWaitProcInterrupt` body that was read instruction by instruction), and the probe is in the tree with its hook already written for whoever wants to hold the carry open. **INVENTORY CORRECTION, because this row's own 2026-08-24 count was wrong:** the `ftcommon` directory holds **83** source TUs, not 78, and **15** are absent, not ten -- `capturecaptain`/`capturekirby`/`captureyoshi` (fighters 3, 10, 7), `get`, five `hammer*`, three `item*`, `dokan` (P2-4), `tarucann` (P2-6) and **`sleep`**. Fourteen of the fifteen belong to content this phase has not reached; `ftcommonitemthrow.c` is partly reimplemented on purpose (above). `ftcommonsleep.c` is the one that does not fit that story and is now board row **P2-3r14**. **`ftcommondokan` is still NOT Mario's entry pipe** -- it drives `nMPMapObjKindDokanL/R`, the stage's own warp pipes. **ALSO ANSWERED, since `dk.md` asked:** Giant Punch has **no release armour** in the source (`ftdonkeyspecialn.c` never touches `knockback_resist_*`; the only passive resist in `ftManagerInitFighter` belongs to Giant DK and Metal Mario), and its charge **persists through damage/hitstun/knockdown and resets only on a KO respawn or on release** -- `ftManagerInitFighter` is the sole zeroing site and its only callers are `ftManagerMakeFighter` and `ftcommonrebirth.c:69`. **TOOLING FIXED ON THE WAY.** (a) `check-architecture.ps1` has been RED since 2026-08-22: its per-fighter allowlist of status-table wrappers that legitimately include a decomp header never got the Luigi and Donkey entries the staging commit added. Both are added, with a note that the list is per fighter. One pre-existing failure remains and is NOT mine to sanction -- `src/import/battleship_ftcommon_entry.c` is a partial reimplementation with no decomp include, owned by the entry rows. The checker is in no verify profile, which is how it stayed red. (b) `probe-trace-symbols.ps1` now prints gdb's `Breakpoint N at ...` lines beside the TRACE markers: it nm-verifies a symbol then breaks on it by NAME, and gdb can answer `Breakpoint 5 at 0x0: <name>. (6 locations)` for an inlined body -- a zero hit count on such a breakpoint is not evidence of absence, and nothing on screen used to say so. (c) New `scripts/probe-dk-cargo.ps1`, described above. **STILL OPEN ON DK'S UNIT:** budgets/CSS/owner feel, and specifically an owner eye on the carried fighter's placement, which comes from `ftCommonCapturePulledRotateScale`'s Euler extraction and has never been looked at on a DS screen. **BOUNDARY NOTE:** `p2_fourcpu_stress` builds `build-p2-fourcpu-tickhud`, which is `NDS_P2_FOUR_CPU_ROSTER 0` / `NDS_P2_DONKEY 0` -- the MIRROR roster. The four-distinct-kind roster is a lab arm (`build-p2-fourcpu-roster4`). So Boundary compiles this change on `p2_shell_loop` and `p2_battle_realtime` (both `NDS_P2_DONKEY 1`) and reaches it at runtime on no arm; the runtime evidence above is the coverage and Boundary is the not-harmed control. **BOUNDARY GREEN ON ALL THREE ARMS** (`verify-all.ps1 -Profile Boundary`, 2026-08-25, exit 0, "Boundary verification profile passed.", **zero `Exception:` lines over 960,866 captured lines**, `artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3r10-boundary.log`): `p2_shell_loop` (`LOOPDONE enters=11 exits=11 rej=0 mism=0 posefull=0`, `LOOPKIT mismatch=0 readfail=0 overflow=0`, `LOOPSURF fireblit=1`), `p2_battle_realtime`, and `p2_fourcpu_stress` -- frames 1..1973, clock 60 -> 1 (98.33%), 0 humans / 4 CPUs / 4 fighter GObjs / mask `0xF`, `syMallocOverflow` 0, `objmanPanic` 0, general-heap low-water **246,292 B** against the 25,600 floor. **The mirror arm is byte-identical to P2-3r13's banked figures** -- `ALL` P50/P95 **1,677,824 / 2,238,208**, `WORK-H` **1,239,808 / 1,787,840**, `animCacheRejects` 18 / misses 31, `dObjActiveMax` 209 -- which is the honest control here rather than a coincidence: that arm is `NDS_P2_DONKEY 0` and therefore compiles the UNCHANGED `#else` branch. Root ROMs unchanged (`smash64ds.nds` `9F612546...11EE`, `smash64ds-battle-playable-hwtri.nds` `32369244...31AC`); nothing was published. |
| P2-3r5 | Owner bug (2026-08-23): "fighters are present before intros. The whole point of intros is that the fighters are 'presented' during their intros" | **fixed 2026-08-24 — owner visual pass pending** | **The port drew every fighter from frame one because the shipping draw path had no invisibility gate at all.** The source's flow: `ftManagerMakeFighter` puts every non-`is_skip_entry` fighter into `nFTCommonStatusEntry` via `ftCommonEntrySetStatus` (ftcommonentry.c:49-56), which sets `is_invisible`; `ifCommonEntryFocusThread` (ifcommon.c:2256) then calls `ftCommonAppearSetStatus` on ONE fighter at a time with sleeps between, and `ftMainSetStatus` clears `is_invisible` as it does (ftmain.c:4462). So a fighter is undrawn from match setup until its own intro reaches it -- which is what makes an intro a presentation rather than a flourish over an already-visible cast. The gate lives in `ftDisplayMainProcDisplay` (ftdisplaymain.c:1087-1092), and on this target that function is REPLACED wholesale: `src/port/reloc_backend_diagnostic_recorders.c` defines its own `ftDisplayMainProcDisplay` that calls `ndsFighterDisplayContractSubmit` under `NDS_RENDERER_HW_TRIANGLES` and never runs the decomp body holding the gate. Every port-side piece was already correct -- `ftCommonEntrySetStatus` is imported and reached, `is_invisible` is set -- only the reader was missing. Fixed at that seam with the source's own two lines. **Evidence** (`artifacts/visibility/2026-08-24_p2-3-introgate-*.png`): at `scVSBattleStartBattle`+8 presents the stage draws with NO fighters; at `ftCommonAppearSetStatus`+8 Mario is emerging from his pipe alone; at +28 Mario stands and FOX is appearing on the platform above -- the source's staggered per-fighter presentation. Shadows need no equivalent because `ftShadowMakeShadow` is still a stub. |
| P2-3r6 | Owner bug (2026-08-23, re-reported 2026-08-24 with screenshots): Mario's green entry pipe is incomplete, leaks Mario/stage through its rim, then ends as a gray/white slab | **FIXED 2026-08-24 — four source-contract defects closed** | The source Special2 animation was already byte-faithful (`ScaY 0->1 over 7`, `TraY ->321.0002 over 18`, hold 50, return over 18, hold 27, then hide), but four DS seams broke what it presented. **(1) Fighter lifetime:** the DS pose clock can still expose `-0.0` on the Appear bind tick, so `ftCommonAppearProcUpdate` could interpret the new status as finished; the source tick counter now gates that test with `status_total_tics > 1`. **(2) Pipe geometry:** Mario's 0x04c0 barrel display list intentionally reuses six RSP vertex-cache slots loaded by the preceding 0x03c0 rim and modifies only their ST. Eighteen emitted corners therefore retain the rim's load-time matrix. The AOT generator now records a matrix-root byte per corner; the native entry owner retains each root's modelview/composed matrix and CPU-projects mixed-cache groups from the recorded load-time matrix, matching the generic renderer's cross-matrix semantics. A true generic control required clearing both `gFTMarioFileSpecial2` and the Luigi Special2 alias; it rendered continuously, which isolated that defect to the native owner. **(3) Terminal visibility:** at source frame 100 the rim animation sets `DOBJ_FLAG_HIDDEN` for 20 frames. BattleShip `gcDrawDObjTree` hides that node *and its child subtree*; the DS effect walker used to suppress only the node and still draw its barrel child, producing the reported gray/white slab. The shared effect-tree walk now preserves the source subtree rule while leaving siblings independent. **(4) Opaque palette index 0 was being discarded:** the entry-effect PAL16 upload reused an IFCommon helper that unconditionally set DS `GL_TEXTURE_COLOR0_TRANSPARENT`. That is valid only when the converted source image actually has alpha-0 texels. Mario's 16x8 pipe texture has opaque-green palette[0]=`0x89e5` and an entire row of index-0 texels; its 32x32 opening texture has opaque-green palette[0]=`0x8120` and 392 index-0 samples. The uploader was therefore punching horizontal/inner-rim holes through valid polygons, exactly matching the owner's `green rim -> Mario fragments -> green body` and background leak beside Mario's ear. The generator now emits a per-texture colour-0 transparency bit from the canonical RGBA5551 repack contract (`palette[0] == 0` only when real transparency exists), and the runtime enables COLOR0 only for those textures. **Fresh evidence:** `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-24_pipe-color0-fix-mario-pipe.png` and its `-4x` crop show only Mario's head above an unbroken green front face with the dark/green inner opening intact; `2026-08-24_pipe-color0-fix-p10-mario-pipe.png` shows the earlier pipe frame solid; `2026-08-24_pipe-color0-fix-hide-fighter-entry-1+104.png` shows no terminal slab. Generator regeneration + `git diff --check` are clean; the fresh one-lap shell gate passes 11 scene entries with zero faults and a 39,432 B arena free floor. Published `smash64ds.nds` is 16,950,272 B, SHA-256 `5f87d60b2924db65a4f40732b8bad4af95824aec1aa9232cbc130700c4c66bf9`. |
| P2-3r3 | Donkey Kong in-flight work (stopped agent) | **crash fixed 2026-08-23 (`00cf338516d`); the `-P2ProofFighter0Kind 2` proof run is in flight** | **The "battle setup" crash was the RESULTS scene.** `scripts/probe-trace-symbols.ps1` (new: prints a marker at each listed symbol and lets a wander run to its ceiling, since melonDS raises no exception for it) placed it in one run: `scVSBattleStartBattle` → both fighters made → **213 `ftMainSetStatus` hits (a complete match)** → `ftManagerAllocFighter` from `ndsMNVSResultsSetupFilesKind`'s caller → `mnVSResultsMakeFighter` → `ftManagerMakeFighter` → wander. `ndsMNVSResultsSetupFilesKind` (`battleship_mnvsresults.c`) loaded files for Mario and Fox ONLY, so Results built a Donkey Kong from files that were never loaded; Luigi would have hit the same wall on his first Results screen. Free-running the same ROM ≤30 s is healthy (fighters made, physics/collision ticking, Fox CPU acting); the wander needs the harness's full ride. History: `smash64ds-battle-playable-fast-hwtri` had **rotted unbuildable** at bare flags before `26936d9218a` (the profile-2/Task56 conflict its own comment names) — that checkpoint's ~40-flag pin restored building, and the checkpoint ALSO rewired the shared natural-combat choreography ungated (Down-tap pass re-arm, `ndsFighterNaturalCombatRecoverTeeter`, Settle walk/run phases in `reloc_backend_movement.c`), so the first-ever exercise of the pinned ROM ran a never-tested fight script. Current suspect: the new choreography deterministically drives the guest into a wandering code path (possibly a latent port defect it exposes); the confirm/deny arm is a temporary revert of the ungated choreography hunks. **DK itself is healthy on the realtime route:** `-P2ProofFighter0Kind 2 -RealtimePresentation` passed its full smoke 2026-08-23 (exit 0, 212 frames, ftr contract 4086/4086, no crash; the known holds-30 warning only). **Route repaired end-to-end (2026-08-23 evening, `57f6511e984`..`f76c16f148f`):** the corpse was a null-page DL-span walk (`gSYTaskmanDLHeads` holds residue on a never-presenting target; `ndsRendererAdapterDisplayProcSpanValid` now fails closed), the frame diagnostics publish from the bounded submit seam, the fast pin runs the battlepack isolation-control arm (`BATTLEPACK=0 KEEP_CACHE=1`, ledger headroom 95,424 → 285,056), the moveset GrabCatch drive reuses the Down-tap pass drive (a rebirth Fall can land the victim on the top platform — attacker sat in Catch 6,125 frames below), the approach recognises arrival-by-contact (DK's source jostle 175 puts DK+Fox push equilibrium at 311.85, over the 240 stop range), and every match-flow/presented-path pin in the gcrunall contract has a bounded-fast consistency arm while realtime targets keep exact pins. **Kind −1 is GREEN end-to-end** (`battle_playable Pupupu ftmanager natural-combat harness passed`, mask 0x7ffff, 2,984 updates). **Kind 2 GREEN (2026-08-23 late night, `9c412271f0f`):** the stall was never collision -- the FTParts-staleness theory is withdrawn as the stall cause (the getter chain self-heals through `unk_dobjtrans_0x10`; a live chain-walk probe reached the fighter root correctly). Actual chain, each proven in the guest: (1) the Attack phase pressed A with the attacker facing AWAY (Fox at the DK jostle-contact equilibrium dx=311.85 with lr=-1) -- the input drive now turns toward the victim before pressing, same gate the projectile phase used; (2) with no Mario-family fighter the projectile actor IS the reflector Fox, so down-B hijacked the fire phase -- the reflector proof now requires distinct slots and disables cleanly; (3) the blaster laser is born and destroyed inside ONE gcRunAll (one 160-unit step, proc_hit 6 damage, lifetime 0), invisible to the per-update sampler -- the blaster now credits its hit seam like the Mario fireball's destroy hooks; (4) DK gets KO'd accidentally mid-moveset, which made StartKOExit skip the driven exit and left BPLAY bit 1 unreachable -- the skip now requires the drive's own evidence and clears stale KO counters; (5) the top-HUD stock min/max is frozen by design on the fast target (display procs paused; probe caught current=5 while the source array read 9) -- that conjunct is scoped off the fast target only. Both bounded proofs pass on one tree: kind 2 (full DK moveset + Giant Punch/Spinning Kong/Hand Slap + blaster projectile + driven KO, mask 0x7ffff, retries 0) and kind -1 (reflector 0xff, specials 0xfff). **Realtime blocker re-decoded:** status 68 is nFTCommonStatusDownBounceU (knockdown bounce, face-up), NOT the Dokan pipe family (62-65) -- the earlier park diagnosis misread the enum. DK enters his own Taru barrel Appear (`battleship_ftcommon_entry.c` handles nFTKindDonkey under NDS_P2_DONKEY). Next: rebuild the realtime DK ROM on this tree and re-observe -- suspect the DownBounceU figatree/anim-end never fires for DK, freezing him mid-knockdown. | **OWNER BATCH 2026-08-23 (six reports).** Landed: the in-progress roster on the character select (Luigi selectable, his portrait dimmed under the source's own question-mark plate; `NDS_P2_SHELL_ROSTER` is a measured level -- roster 1 leaves 57,136 B of arena headroom, roster 2 leaves 13,840 and the battle aborts in `ifCommonCountdownMakeInterface`, so DK waits for the per-fighter native-owner tables to move out of the ARM9 image); costume cycling by pressing A on a slot's 3D preview (source ids, source used-costume rule, Team Battle refuses); pose slots released on fighter destroy (`ndsFtPoseUnbind` had one caller and never ran on a CSS rebuild); the BGM no longer dies when a fighter's files load (suspend/resume fence -- the stall was a measured seam miss + error stop); walk dwell steps counted so the input-ring invariant stays exact. Boundary green on all three arms, freeplay republished. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND for two more reports** ("fighters are present before intros" and "the pipe shows only the rim"): the Appear motion attaches with `anim_frame = 0`, so `ftCommonAppearProcUpdate`'s `anim_frame <= 0` test fires on the first update and the fighter goes straight to Wait -- the intro is skipped entirely while the pipe effect plays its own animation beside it. Measured live: `POSTAPPEAR status=222 motion=197 animframe=0.000000`, then `ftCommonWaitSetStatus` immediately, with `entry_pos` still (0,0). The pipe itself is NOT the defect -- per-root counts are m0=50 (rim) m1=60 (barrel), fallback 0, so both roots submit natively; the barrel's absence follows from the entry never rising. **CORRECTION, same night, before anyone acts on the above:** intros are NOT globally skipped. With fighter identity attached to the timeline, fighter A enters Appear at frame 2886 and its `anim_frame` reads 21.000000 eleven frames later -- its animation is running normally. The `animframe=0` reading that suggested a zero-length motion was sampled immediately after `ftCommonAppearSetStatus` returns, before the first pose evaluation publishes the clock, so it measured nothing. The fighter-animation loader is also exonerated by its new counters: resolved=23, fallback=0 over a full walk into the battle (`gNdsRelocForceFighterAnimResolveCount` / `...FallbackCount`, added because that arm silently handed back the raw heap). THE REMAINING FACT is narrower and still unexplained: `ftCommonWaitSetStatus` lands on fighter B on the very frame B enters Appear (both at 2897), while A is mid-animation. Next: a per-fighter status timeline across the entry window -- who calls Wait on B, and whether B's Appear is a second entry from `ifCommonEntryFocusThread` rather than its first. The pipe is unrelated to all of this: both its roots submit (m0=50 rim, m1=60 barrel, fallback 0).
| P2-3r12 | Boundary battle arm RED on the BGM ADPCM guard: one seam miss and one error stop | **FIXED 2026-08-25** | **Two defects, in opposite directions, and BattleShip settles both.** **(1) HEAD's 2,209 suspends were a source-fidelity defect, not an expensive fence.** `ndsMenuShellCssSyncPreviews` calls `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewSync` for all FOUR slots on EVERY character-select tic, and the port's `update_fighter` predicate treated `fighter_gobj == NULL` as "needs a rebuild" — permanently true for the two N/A slots, because the updater makes nothing for them and the slot is still NULL next tic. The source says that call is a no-op: `mnPlayersVSMakeFighter` (`decomp/.../mn/mnplayers/mnplayersvs.c:1624`) wraps its ENTIRE body in `if (fkind != nFTKindNull)`, and `mnPlayersVSUpdateFighter`'s (`:2337`) hide-and-skip branch requires a non-NULL `fighter_gobj`, so for an empty slot the source updater provably does nothing at all — and BattleShip only calls it from four EVENT sites (`:2492` kind-select, `:2942` cursor-grab, `:3564` puck, `:4210`/`:4242`), never per tic. Adding `&& (fkind != nFTKindNull)` to the creation term is source-exact, not an optimisation, and it deletes 2,209 no-op rebuilds per visit. That — not the fence's cost — was the whole of the "audible song lurch". **(2) The residency predicate's claim was false, and its falsifier watched the wrong counter.** `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewCanRebuildWithoutIO` returned TRUE for every prepared kind, so `gNdsAudioBgmBlockingSuspendCount` stayed 0 for a whole run — while the visit still ran **+1,054 `gNdsRelocAssetPayloadReadCount`** with BGM live, which is what missed the ADPCM seam. Its post-hoc revoke compared `gNdsRelocAssetHeaderReadCount` (207 → 207, unchanged), `gNdsR2AnimCacheMisses` and `gNdsNativeOwnerImageLoadCount` (both unchanged) — never the PAYLOAD counter, the one that measures real blocking traffic — so it could not fire. Predicate and its `sNdsPlayersVSPreviewResidentKindMask` are DELETED; the fence is unconditional on a genuine rebuild again, and cheap now that (1) stopped manufacturing them. **Localisation, so the warming pass is not blamed for this:** bracketing `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewInit` on BOTH character-select entries shows its burst is innocent — hdr 109→207, payload 1637→1675, ownerload 4→8, `seammiss=0` at its exit both times; the miss lands later in the visit with the +1,054 payload burst. `ndsMNPlayersVSPreviewPrepareResidentKinds` therefore STAYS (it really does warm core files, submotion-0 figatrees and the P2-3 owner images, and it reduces what the fence must cover); what it does not license is skipping the fence. **Measured, one probe, same scripted walk, eleven scene boundaries per ROM:** | arm | suspend/resume per CSS visit | seammiss / error / overrun | |---|---|---| | HEAD (`build-p2-shell-bgmctl`) | 2,209 | 0 / 0 / 0 | | working tree before | 0 (never fenced) | **1 / 1 / 1** | | after this row | **12** | **0 / 0 / 0** | 12 per visit is one per genuine rebuild event in the walk — the order of a load burst, 184x below HEAD — and `suspend` steps 0→12 at the first PlayersVS and 12→24 at the second. Evidence: `artifacts/verifier-temp/slot7/bgm_scenes_{ctl,wt,r12fix}.gdb.out`, `…/bgm_when_wt2.gdb.out` for the PreviewInit bracket, `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-25_p2-3r12-css-css-default*.png` for the unchanged screen. **Trap recorded twice over, both cost a run:** `scripts/menus/probe-p2-shell.ps1` cannot drive a HEAD-source ROM at all — its `CSSRES` marker reads `gNdsPlayersVSPreviewResident*Mask`, which the uncommitted file ADDS, and gdb fails that one printf then aborts the whole sourced command file after the first stop, leaving a capture that merely looks short; any control arm for a dirty-tree defect needs a probe restricted to symbols BOTH arms have. And `watch <global>` is not available here: melonDS's stub has no hardware watchpoint, gdb silently falls back to a SOFTWARE one, and that single-steps the entire guest — the run makes no measurable progress and exits by timeout. Bracket with breakpoints instead. **Follow-up, not this row:** a rebuild is still not storage-free (+1,054 payload reads per visit behind the fence). `gNdsRelocAssetPayloadReadCount` is the counter that work is judged by. **Boundary GREEN on all three arms** after the fix (`verify-all.ps1 -Profile Boundary`, 2026-08-25, exit 0, zero exceptions): `p2_shell_loop`, `p2_battle_realtime` -- the arm this row's red belonged to -- and `p2_fourcpu_stress`. **Practice item, from a hazard this row walked into:** `Stop-Process -Force` on melonDS skips `Restore-MelonDSGdbConfig` and the emulator's own SD flush, and a corrupt `emulators/melonds/dldi.bin` then reads as a harness TIMEOUT rather than a crash (three cycles lost to that before, 2026-08-14). Prefer letting a harness finish or time out; after any unavoidable forced kill, treat `dldi.bin` as suspect and verify it before the next measured run -- its FAT boot signature `0x55 0xAA` at offsets 510/511 is the cheap check, and size alone proves nothing because a zeroed image is still 536,870,912 bytes. Better still, make it automatic at the seam that already owns emulator teardown (`scripts/lib/melonds.ps1`); not done here, and it is not this row's scope. |
| P2-3f1 | The two fighter-shaped static checkers were both RED and in NO verify profile | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- both green, both now run unconditionally inside `verify-all.ps1`, and the concept one of them could not express is now expressible** | **A CHECKER NOBODY RUNS PROTECTS NOTHING, AND ONE OF THESE WAS HIDING A SECOND FAILURE BEHIND ITS FIRST.** `check-architecture.ps1` sets `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'` and reported failures with `Write-Error` **inside a loop**, so it terminated on failure #1 and its own `throw` never ran: every doc that described it (P2-3r10, P2-3r14) recorded "one pre-existing failure" and there were **two** -- `battleship_ftcommon_entry.c` AND `battleship_ftcommon_itemthrow.c`. It prints the whole list on the normal stream now, then throws. **The rule could not express what those two files are.** Both are *bounded partial imports*: the reachable half of a source TU transcribed verbatim, the unreachable half deliberately left to the phase that lands its content, because importing whole would drag in Appear status enums and nine entry-effect makers for fighters that have 16-entry stub status tables (`ftcommonentry.c`), or the whole item manager into an items-off build (`ftcommonitemthrow.c`). That is a position, not sloppiness -- but it has to be declared. The rule is now two-sided: the path is registered in the checker AND the file carries `NDS_PARTIAL_IMPORT: <decomp source path>` naming exactly the registered TU. A comment alone cannot buy the exception (an unregistered file carrying the marker reds) and a registered file that drops or renames its marker reds. **Both negative controls were run and both fired** (2026-08-25): marker removed from the registered file -> `registered bounded partial import is missing its in-file ... marker`; bogus marker added to unregistered `battleship_ftcommon_dash.c` -> `undeclared bounded partial import`, and **both printed together**, which the old reporting could not have done. **`check-decomp-header-mirror.py` had two real disagreements and both are now corrected toward the source.** (1) `FTSTAT_OPENING1_START` was `0xE0000` in `include/ft/fighter.h` against decomp `ft/ftdef.h:20`'s `0x1000F`. The only reader is the imported `ftmain.c:4554` status-table ladder, so the port's value was the live one: with the larger threshold every Opening-1 status falls into the Opening-2 arm and indexes `D_ovl1_80390BE8` instead of `D_ovl1_80390D20[fkind]`. Unreachable today (no live status id survives the `FTSTAT_CHARDATA_START` subtraction above 0x1000F; the opening cinematic is P2-7) -- latent, not live. (2) `nSYAudioBGMExplain` was a placeholder `0` in `include/sys/audio.h` against decomp `gmMusicID`'s 34. `gmMusicID` has **no region conditionals**, so the ordinal is unambiguous, and the count is cross-checked by two figures this tree already banked from the same enum (ModeSelect 44, row P2-1d-1; BattleSelect 10, P2-1e-1). 0 collides with `nSYAudioBGMPupupu`, so the imported `mnTitleProceedDemoNext` (`mntitle.c:469`, the `nSCKindExplain` arm) would have started **Dream Land's music** on leaving How-to-Play; that arm needs the P2-7 attract flow, so again latent. Neither symbol has any other reader in `src/` or `include/` (checked `--no-ignore`). **Both are now in `verify-all.ps1`'s unconditional static block** (`$expectedVerifiers` 4 -> 6), which meant teaching `Invoke-VerifyScriptOnce` to dispatch on file extension so a `.py` checker can be registered without a wrapper `.ps1` existing only to shell out to it. `check-melonds-policy.ps1` green on the changed `Start-Process`. **FOUND WHILE DOING THIS AND FIXED IN ITS OWN ROW: P2-3f2 -- Luigi has no entry.** **TWO ONE-LINERS FOR WHOEVER IS NEXT, NOT DONE HERE.** (a) `check-docs.ps1` is invoked by NOTHING -- it is green, but it is the checker that enforces the `HANDOFF.md` 200-line cap and the docs registry, and an unrun green checker becomes an unrun red one. It belongs in the same static block; it was left out only because adding it mid-run would have invalidated this row's Boundary provenance. (b) **You cannot bank a Boundary log with `Tee-Object`.** `Invoke-VerifyScriptOnce` replays each child verifier's captured output with `[Console]::Out.Write`, which bypasses the PowerShell pipeline, so `.\scriptserify-all.ps1 -Profile Boundary *>&1 | Tee-Object -FilePath x.log` writes a **five-line** file that looks like a complete pass. Redirect the whole process instead. |

It needed neither. **`0xe2` (`G_SETOTHERMODE_L`) needed ZERO runtime code:** it maps to the existing effect 2 (`NDS_NATIVE_STATE_OTHERMODE`) because `ndsRendererNativeApplyStateDelta` passes `delta->w0 >> 24` to `ndsRendererRecordOtherMode`, which has always dispatched on the opcode byte and carries an explicit `NDS_RENDERER_OP_SETOTHERMODE_L` arm (`nds_renderer.c:8400`) accumulating othermode_L through the same shift/len bitfield decode the source uses -- byte-for-byte what `sm64-nds`'s `g_setothermode_l` (`src/nds/nds_renderer.c:725`) does, which is the inspect-the-DS-references rule paying off by DELETING the work. **`0xf9` (`G_SETBLENDCOLOR`) needed ONE `case`:** effect 12 (`NDS_NATIVE_STATE_BLEND`) already existed for the native STAGE program (`NDS_TASK26_BLEND`); the FIGHTER applier and `ndsRendererValidateNativeStateSpan` simply had no arm for it, because no fighter owner had ever produced one. **AND THE DS ANSWER WAS ALREADY WIRED:** `ndsRendererHardwareApplyAlphaTest` reads exactly these two fields and, on `G_AC_THRESHOLD`, emits `glEnable(GL_ALPHA_TEST)` + `glAlphaFunc(blend_color.a >> 4)`; Falcon's blend colour is `(0,0,0,0)` so the reference is 0 -- pass when alpha > 0, the plain binary cutout the source asks for. **No fidelity trade, so nothing to record under the sacrifice order and no owner visual pass owed.** (`sm64-nds` throws `G_SETBLENDCOLOR` away, `:1017`; this port keeps it because it IS the alpha reference the threshold compares against.) **EVIDENCE, and the control matters more than the result:** Falcon's high-detail model now decodes clean -- 17 roots, 87 state deltas, 254 sequence entries, 34 vertex actions, 319 triangles, 34 runs, 34 epochs, the five commands landing as deltas 54/55 (OTHERMODE), 56 (BLEND), 60/61 (OTHERMODE restore) -- while the **LOW**-detail decode reproduces P2-3f4's pre-change figures EXACTLY (73/223/30/200/30/30), which is what proves the two opcodes changed nothing else; and `generate_nds_native_owners.py --check` passes, so `nds_native_fighter_owner.generated.inc` and the consumed-fields manifest are BYTE-IDENTICAL and Mario/Fox/Luigi/Donkey are untouched (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f/captain-high-detail-decode.txt`). **THE RUNTIME SLICE, and it is bigger than the blocker was:** `NDS_P2_CAPTAIN` flag + status-table shim (in `check-architecture.ps1`'s per-fighter allowlist); `battleship_captain.c` importing `ftcaptainspecialn/lw/hi.c` verbatim; a NEW `battleship_ftcommon_capturecaptain.c` for Falcon Dive's VICTIM side (one of the fifteen absent `ftcommon` TUs P2-3r10 counted, and an ftcommon TU on purpose -- `nFTCommonStatusCaptureCaptain` is a status ANY fighter can end up in); the Falcon branch in the bounded `battleship_ftcommon_entry.c` import with his two-status Appear ladder, `is_rotate` on `lr == -1`, `efManagerCaptainEntryCarMakeEffect` and the `ftParamMoveDLLink(gobj, 1)` tail; and `HALF_PI32` mirrored into `include/macros.h`. **THE TWO `mpcommon` SEAMS LANDED AT THE SEAM, NOT IN THE FIGHTER, and both were worse than 'missing':** `mpCommonCheckFighterCeilHeavyCliff` was DECLARED in `include/mp/map.h` with no definition anywhere in the tree -- a link error waiting for its first caller -- and `mpCommonSetFighterWaitOrLanding` did not exist at all, its three lines having been COPIED INLINE into `mpCommonProcFighterWaitOrLanding` and `mpCommonProcFighterCliffFloorCeil`. Both now live in `reloc_backend_compat_shims.c`, `mpCommonProcFighterCliffWaitOrLanding` is the source one-liner over them, and the duplicate body is deleted; Kirby, Purin, Link and Yoshi all reach the same pair. **I PUSHED BACK ON THE BRIEF ON ONE POINT AND THE ELF SETTLED IT: THE WEAK TWINS MUST NOT BE DELETED.** Both this row's brief and P2-3f4's own remainder list said to delete the six `NDS_SPECIAL_COMMON_WEAK_STATUS` / `NDS_REFLECTOR_WEAK_STATUS` twins and called that "the Donkey Kong pattern verbatim". DK did not do that -- `battleship_special_common.c` still carries `ftDonkeySpecialNStartSetStatus` and three siblings as weak -- and it CANNOT: the dispatch tables in the imported `ftcommonspecialn.c` name every fighter's setter UNCONDITIONALLY, so a `NDS_P2_CAPTAIN=0` build needs the twin to link. The pattern is *strong definition beside weak twin, linker picks strong*, and the check is `nm`, never a grep of `src/`. **VERIFIED ON THE LINKED ELF** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f/captain-runtime-slice-linked-elf.txt`, `make TARGET=smash64ds BUILD=build-p2-3f5-captain NDS_P2_CAPTAIN=1`): all six `ftCaptainSpecial*SetStatus` are `T` with not one `W` left; 42 strong `ftCaptain*` bodies; `dFTCaptainSpecialStatusDescs` is `0x17c` = 19 entries and not the 16-entry `NDS_FT_STATUS_STUB16`; `efManagerCaptainEntryCar`/`FalconPunch`/`FalconKick` are IN (all three had been gc-sectioned away for want of a caller); the three `ftCommonCaptureCaptain*` bodies replaced the weak inactive stub; and the Attack13 aliases disassemble to `bl ndsBaseFTCommonAttack13Proc*`. **`ftCommonAttack13Proc{Update,Interrupt}` had to be added to `battleship_ftstatus_callback_aliases.c` beside Attack11/12 -- Attack13 is the ONE jab step SSB64 makes character-specific, so it sits in each fighter's own special table and no landed fighter had ever named it. The remaining eight fighters all need the same pair.** **ONE SOURCE CONSTANT IS DELIBERATELY NOT VERBATIM:** `FTCAPTAIN_FALCONPUNCH_VEL_MUL` is `0.92` -- a DOUBLE -- in `ftcaptain.h`, so `vel_air.y *= 0.92` compiles to two soft-float conversions plus an `aeabi` double multiply per axis per aerial-punch tick on this Thumb ARM9 build; restated `0.92F`. **WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM:** no shipped configuration sets `NDS_P2_CAPTAIN`, so Boundary builds `NDS_P2_CAPTAIN=0` and proves only that the slice costs the shipped ROM nothing. Falcon still needs a native owner, the ~40 `#if NDS_P2_DONKEY` admission sites, audio ordinals and an arena measurement before he is selectable -- ordered in `falcon.md`. **TWO OPEN QUESTIONS FOUND AND LEFT OPEN ON PURPOSE:** (a) the port's `mpCommonProcFighterProject` calls `mpCommonRunFighterCollisionDefault` where the source calls `mpProcessUpdateMain(..., MAP_PROC_TYPE_PROJECT)`; the faithful `mpCommonCheckFighterProject` already exists one line away, but it is a SHARED seam every status table's `ProcMap` can reach, so it gets its own row with a regression run rather than a drive-by inside a fighter landing (Falcon Dive uses it for a 15-tick window); (b) `gFTDataCaptainMainMotion` is NULL in every built configuration, so the Falcon Dive victim tether would dereference a NULL the first time a Falcon is spawned WITHOUT his asset graph -- the admission row must land assets before it lands the CSS entry. **BOUNDARY GREEN ON ALL THREE ARMS** (`verify-all.ps1 -Profile Boundary`, 2026-08-25, exit 0, "Boundary verification profile passed.", **zero `Exception:` lines over 2,562 captured lines**, `artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f/boundary-p2-3f5-final.log`, redirected at the process rather than tee'd): `p2_shell_loop` (1 lap, 11 scene entries, per-kind high-waters flat, free floor 258,568 B, `LOOPDONE budget=1`, zero faults), `p2_battle_realtime` (frames 212), `p2_fourcpu_stress` (correctness, cadence, native-owner and memory gates). The FIRST attempt went red on an unrelated harness instrument -- board row **P2-3f7**. **Published `smash64ds.nds` rebuilt from the plain target: 17,013,760 B, SHA-256 `069BFD64...9BC4`** (config asserted from `builds/build/nds_build_config.h`: `NDS_P2_CAPTAIN 0`, `NDS_P2_LUIGI 1`, `NDS_P2_DONKEY 1`, `NDS_P2_MENU_WALK 0`), and `nm` on the published ELF confirms the Captain setters are back to `W` stubs -- the slice costs the shipped ROM nothing. **`verify-all -Profile Boundary` does NOT build `smash64ds`** (no arm targets it), so the root pair has to be removed and rebuilt by hand after any lab build that wrote it; a lab ROM sitting at the published path passes `check-published-roms.ps1`, which checks the contract, not the flags. |
| P2-3f6 | `check-docs.ps1` was invoked by NOTHING -- the one-liner P2-3f1 left open | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- it is in `verify-all.ps1`'s unconditional static block (`$expectedVerifiers` 6 -> 7) and green** | **AN UNRUN GREEN CHECKER BECOMES AN UNRUN RED ONE, AND THIS ONE HAD ALREADY DONE IT ONCE.** `check-docs.ps1` owns the `HANDOFF.md` 200-line cap, the `docs/README.md` index, the board's `## Standing rules` / `## Queue` / one-minute / SHA-256 / Publish-law tokens, AGENTS.md's section caps, the `NDS_ARCH_STUB`/`DEFERRED` marker registry and the rule that every Boundary entry is named in an active doc -- and nothing invoked it. It had been silently dead before: it required two P1-era planning surfaces the P2 restructure archived, so it threw on its first loop and every assertion below that point was unreachable until P2-1M noticed. P2-3f1 found it unwired and left it deliberately, because adding a verifier mid-run would have invalidated that row's Boundary provenance; that reason expired with the row. Static, sub-second, no ROM. Green at wiring time (docs=24, registryEntries=6, AGENTS.md=197 lines) and green in the Boundary run below. |
| P2-3f7 | A one-shot gdb `set variable` is undone by the next cache-line write-back -- `p2_shell_loop`'s budget poke evaporated mid-run | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- the poke is re-applied at every scene-entry stop, and the readback is now deterministic by construction** | **THE GDB STUB WRITES MAIN RAM; THE ARM9 KEEPS WRITING ITS DCACHE.** Found by P2-3f5's first Boundary run, which went red on `BUDGET: gNdsMenuShellWalkBudget=20, requested 1. The poke did not land` -- while the SAME run's capture artifact recorded `LOOPBUDGET budget=1` at the first stop. Both readings are correct: gdb wrote RAM and read RAM back immediately (1), and by the end of the run RAM held 20 again. `gNdsMenuShellWalkBudget` is at `0x0212c374` and `gNdsMenuShellScreen` at `0x0212c378` -- **the same 32-byte cache line** -- and `nds_menu_shell.c:4511` writes the screen on every transition, so the line is dirty with the CPU's own pre-poke copy and the next eviction writes all 32 bytes back over gdb's word. `volatile` does not help: it forces a LOAD, and the load hits the dirty line. **The previous run passed on layout luck** -- P2-3f5's unrelated additions moved the 32-byte boundary so that the two globals, declared 50 lines apart in one file and adjacent in `.data` for months, finally landed in one line together. **Fixed by re-poking at EVERY `ndsSceneManagerEnter` stop instead of only the first** (the `LOOPBUDGET` echo still prints once, so the artifact is unchanged in shape). That makes the end-of-run readback deterministic rather than lucky: the run ENDS at a breakpoint stop, so the last poke and `LOOPDONE`'s read are separated by no guest instruction at all. **RAIL, and it generalises past this harness: a one-shot gdb poke of a guest global is only as durable as its cache line is clean. Re-poke, or poke at the stop you read at.** This sits beside the existing byte-write rail (a 1-byte write to a 4-byte-aligned guest address kills melonDS; use the aligned read-modify-write helper in `scripts/lib/gdb-markers.ps1`) -- same root cause, opposite symptom. Nothing else in the failing run was wrong: arena free floor 258,568 B, 11 scene entries, per-kind high-waters flat, lap pattern exact, zero faults, both before and after. Extracts: `artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f/boundary-p2-3f7-budget-poke-red.txt` (the red run) and `boundary-p2-3f5-final.log` (the green one that superseded it). |
| P2-3f8 | Captain Falcon (roster #3): the native owner, then admission -- is he selectable, and what does he cost? | **LANDED 2026-08-25. He has NO AUDIO -- not one cue is packed -- and the four-kind budget question he opens is NOT closed here. `docs/p2/fighters/falcon.md` carries both.** | **TWO OF THE OWNER GENERATOR'S ASSUMPTIONS WERE HIS TO BREAK, AND ONE OF THEM WAS AN ASSERTION THAT CALLED ITSELF UNREACHABLE.** (1) **Falcon has ZERO cross-matrix runs in either detail** -- every one of his 319 high / 200 low source triangles is single-binding and draws under the current root, so `OWNER_CROSS_BINDING_SLOTS["captain"]` is empty, his GX plan is `(1, 6, 6, 0, 0)` in both details and he claims no physical palette slot at all. | P2-3r17 | Owner bug (2026-08-25): fighters have open seams / holes -- "specific triangles intermittently not rendered" around DK's temple, hands, feet and tie, and the same failure, smaller, at Mario's cap front | **DEFERRED 2026-08-25 BY THE OWNER** (*"let's not spend too much time on it, it can be deferred since adding to the game is more important at the moment"*). **NOT FIXED.
