It just makes the inside surfaces draw so it 'looks' fixed as far as colors go, but the seams are still there."* **THE TRAP, WHICH COST THIS SESSION A FALSE POSITIVE: `NDS_LAB_NO_CULL` changes the picture without closing the holes.** I read arm 1 as closing DK's brow/ear seam and had to withdraw it -- the two frames were at different poses. Never judge a cull arm by "does it look better"; judge only on whether the seam is GONE. **AND WALL-CLOCK CAPTURE CANNOT A/B THIS ROW AT ALL:** eight captures, one binary, two fixed `-DelaySeconds`, every same-delay pair **237,596..536,927 differing pixels of a 570,570-pixel top screen** -- the pose drifts run to run by far more than the defect. A frame lock (`capture-cut-g-exact-frames.ps1 -ExactTimeRemain`) is mandatory, and it refuses `-RendererFastRunMode`/`-SetGlobals` today. **THE SHIPPED FIGHTER DRAW IS A DMA PACKET REPLAY AND IT SWALLOWS LAB PROBES -- including the one this row's own "next experiment" prescribed.** With `NDS_R2_FIGHTER_PACKET=1` (default since 2026-08-23) `ndsFighterPacketTryReplay` returns before the production execute, and the recorder tees `state->texture_prepare_poly_fmt` -- the UNPATCHED value -- into the packet before the begin-batch site a probe patches. **Measured: on a packet build arm 1 (POLY_CULL_NONE) and arm 2 (POLY_CULL_FRONT) render BYTE-IDENTICAL frames; on `NDS_R2_FIGHTER_PACKET=0` arm 2 renders DK visibly inside-out** (`2026-08-25_p2-3r17-nopacket-arm2-cullfront.png`). Extending `NDS_LAB_NO_CULL` to `ndsRendererNativeBeginDirectBatch`, as this row prescribed, would have been silently useless -- the exact failure it was written to avoid, one layer deeper. It is now a build error. **FOUR NEW OFFLINE ORACLES, ALL GREEN, ALL FOUR OWNERS x BOTH DETAILS (6,171 corners):** (1) *VERTEX closure* -- every emitted corner carries its own source vertex's exact position, texcoord and normal, re-derived by an independent O2R walk maintaining the real 32-entry cache including `G_MODIFYVTX`. The previous closure compared CACHE-SLOT TRIPLES and could not see which vertex a slot held, so **per-vertex `cache_slot` reuse across runs is dead at the data level**. (2) *MATRIX-ROUTING closure* -- every corner's baked GX palette slot is the slot its own vertex's joint binding owns; raw corners carry no slot bits. (3) *FACING closure* -- **0** triangles whose geometric normal opposes their own vertex normals; the old winding closure only proved neighbours agree with each other, which a wholly inside-out island passes. (4) *Fixed-point* -- all six shipped `sNdsNativeFighter*PreparedDense*` arrays (541/420/315/201/264/181 rows) equal `pack_fifo_vertex16` of the source integers exactly; the pack is `x*16` on integer coordinates, lossless by construction, **no saturation and no truncation**. **ALSO ELIMINATED:** the `matrix_binding >= input_count -> root_index` silent fallback is **not in the draw path** -- it is inside `ndsRendererScreenSpaceCensusFighterRun`, gated on `gNdsRendererScreenSpaceCensusArmed`. And the Task 56 strips are innocent again on the live path: with `gNdsR2FighterStripRoute=0` the seam is unchanged. **READ FROM SOURCE, NOT MEASURED, AND IT COMPROMISES THIS ROW'S OWN CONTROL ARM:** `reloc_backend_renderer_dl.c:17711` normalizes `config.initial_geometry_mode` from the legacy F3D cull bits (0x1000/0x2000) to the renderer's internal bits (0x200/0x400) **only when `native_root_enabled`**. On the generic path the legacy bits survive, `ndsRendererHardwarePolyFmt` tests 0x200/0x400 and cannot see them, so it emits `POLY_CULL_NONE`. **Mode 0 therefore appears to draw fighters TWO-SIDED while mode 9 honours the source's `G_CULL_BACK`** -- and the original does cull (`decomp/BattleShip-main/decomp/src/ft/ftdisplaymain.c:1177`, `:233`). So "mode 0 draws the head whole" is not a clean reference for anything cull-related. Worth settling on its own merits; measure it before acting on it. **OPEN LEAD, AS THE OWNER NOW READS IT** (*"it just seems like a vertex placement issue"*): individual vertices receiving wrong VALUES -- a collapse gives a zero-area triangle that reads as a missing one (ear, cap, hand, foot), a flyaway gives the stretched spikes in the owner's first screenshots. One mechanism, two signatures, and it explains why cull-none does nothing (a degenerate triangle has no area to face either way). **The data side is now fully exonerated, so the remaining surface is the runtime matrices**: the native path composes each root's world ON THE GEOMETRY ENGINE (`NDS_R2_FIGHTER_GX_COMPOSE=1`, MTX_MULT4x3 chains from a restored parent slot) while mode 0 composes on the CPU -- the one vertex-placement difference left between the two arms of the A/B. **CHEAPEST FIRST EXPERIMENT FOR WHOEVER PICKS THIS UP:** `NDS_R2_FIGHTER_GX_COMPOSE=2` already exists and is exactly this oracle -- it runs BOTH composes and compares them per binding, with `gNdsR2GxComposeVerifyFail` as the counter. One build, one gdb read, no screenshot and no pose to match. (The proof target `override`s the flag to 1, so it needs a lab target or a temporary Makefile edit.) Then diff transformed vertex positions between mode 9 and mode 0 for one affected root -- **Mario's cap is the smallest case and he carries in-binary tables**. **LANDED HERE:** the merged oracle `scripts/fighters/check_native_owner_geometry_closure.py` -- six closures, every landed owner, both detail levels, absorbing and replacing `check_fighter_primitive_streams.py`, which could only ever build the frozen Mario/Fox HIGH context and is why a defect this visible survived every gate; `build.ps1` runs it. The seam probe now reaches the production owner's batch and fails the build on a packet configuration. `capture-melonds.ps1` gained `-SetGlobals` (one GDB session, so a lab route global can be poked at boot *with* the camera or Fox selectors), its single-session guard now covers `-RendererFastRunMode` and `-SetGlobals` and not just `-FoxCpuMode`, and `-RendererFastRunMode` accepts 9 -- the SHIPPING mode, which it had refused. The stale-`poly_fmt` defect is repaired separately and **does not close this row**: measured that it cannot currently fire (a cull change arrives as `NDS_NATIVE_STATE_GEOMETRY`, which invalidates the prepare; Donkey High has one `CULL_NONE` epoch and Mario High no geometry deltas at all). One more recorded, not repaired: `derive_direct_epoch_policies` computes `cull_on = (w1 & 0x400) != 0` from the SET mask alone, so any GEOMETRY delta setting another bit reads as "culling off" -- correct on today's data and it errs toward `CULL_NONE`, which the runtime validation rejects into a fallback rather than mis-drawing. |
| P2-3f9 | The shipped game can be put into a state that does not fit in RAM: a player picks the four heaviest kinds and the ARM9 halts forever | **FIXED 2026-08-25 -- measured from the SHELL first, then fixed with two correctness-safe reclaims totalling 378,048 B; the stress arm is re-pointed to the real argmax** | **IT HALTS, AND THE ROW THAT PREDICTED IT WAS RIGHT IN DIRECTION AND WRONG IN SIZE.** P2-3f8 closed on an INFERENCE -- argmax 335,824 B against P2-3r13's measured 24,356 B margin, so "+46,112 B over" -- and nobody had ever driven four distinct kinds through the shipped menus at any roster. Two new instruments make that runnable: `NDS_P2_SHELL_ARGMAX_ROSTER=1` (Makefile) seeds the P2-1a descriptor with Luigi human + Fox/Captain/Donkey level-3 CPUs so the character select OPENS on the argmax, and swaps the CSS walk for one that presses START without disturbing the four seeded slots (the canonical tour would re-roll slot 3 through `mnPlayersVSRandFighterKind`); `scripts/probe-shell-four-kind.ps1` (new) breaks on `ndsMenuShellCssCommit` / `ftManagerSetupFilesMainKind` / `ftManagerMakeFighter` / `ndsSyMallocOverflowHalt` and lets the real walk drive title -> menus -> CSS -> stage select -> battle. **THE ANSWER** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f9-shell-argmax-run1.txt`): committed roster `s0=0x040003 s1=0x010103 s2=0x070103 s3=0x020103` = Luigi/Fox/Captain/Donkey all at level 3; all four kinds' trees load in the battle scene (Luigi 55,008 -> Fox 116,752 -> Captain 100,160 -> Donkey 77,360); then `ndsRendererNativeEnsureOwnerImage(owner_slot=1, use_low_detail=0)` asks `syMallocSet` for **12,140 B with 5,824 B free** and reaches `ndsSyMallocOverflowHalt`. `batt=0` -- `ifCommonBattleUpdateInterfaceAll` never runs, so the match never starts and the ARM9 spins forever with no error. **Deficit at the failing instruction: 6,316 B, not 46,112.** The inference's error is instructive: it compared a per-kind total against a margin measured on a DIFFERENT ARM, and it is the arm difference that mattered most. **THE SHELL'S ARENA IS SMALLER THAN THE LAB'S, WHICH NO BANKED FIGURE CARRIED.** `gNdsTaskmanArenaChosenSize` reads **1,658,880 with AllocFail 18** on the shell target, against `p2_fourcpu_stress`'s 1,695,744 (AllocFail 9) and the mirror arm's 1,732,608 (AllocFail 0) -- the shell's larger ARM9 binary costs 36,864 B more arena than the stress arm and 73,728 B more than the mirror, because `ndsTaskmanArenaBytes` steps `calloc` down 4,096 at a time and only the chosen size records the difference. Every four-kind projection made from the lab arm was therefore optimistic by up to 73,728 B before any roster question. **AND THE CHARACTER SELECT ALREADY HOLDS ALL FIVE KINDS AT ONCE, COMFORTABLY.** In scene 16 the preview path runs `ftManagerSetupFilesAllKind` for every selectable kind -- Mario 51,952, Fox 116,752, Luigi 41,552, Donkey 77,360, Captain 100,160 -- and the screen still commits with **905,328 B free**. The shortage is entirely in the battle scene, which is the opposite of where the per-kind table alone would point. **THE FIX CAME OUT OF A CENSUS, NOT A THEORY.** `probe-shell-four-kind.ps1 -TraceMallocAtLeast 16384` names every scene-arena allocation with its backtrace, and the battle scene's list is short: 208,672 (`scVSBattleSetupFiles`), 202,816 (`mpCollisionInitGroundData` -- Dream Land), 52,736 (`efManagerInitEffects`), 33,152, 28,352, the four kind trees, and **one request of 451,776 B that nothing had ever attributed -- 27.2% of the whole arena**. It is `NDS_R2_ANIM_CACHE_ARENA_BYTES`: 287,936 B of BattlePack reserve plus 163,840 B of raw animation cache. **LEVER 1: THE GRAPHICS HEAP, P2-3r13'S OWN UNTAKEN ONE, NOW BOUNDED RATHER THAN SAMPLED (+90,112 B).** `NDS_R2_VSBATTLE_GRAPHICS_ARENA_BYTES` 0xD000 -> 0x2000, two contexts. r13 measured a 96 B per-frame peak of 53,248 and declined to cut because one match's sample is not a bound and three writers were not in it; this row bounded them from the SOURCE instead. Afterimage vertices (`ftdisplaymain.c:457-581`) are capped by `FTAfterImage desc[3]` -- at most 3 segments x 2 Vtx plus 2 joins x 6 interpolation steps x 2 Vtx = 30 Vtx = **480 B**, and the fighter draw restores the heap pointer around itself so four fighters do not stack. The fighter light is one 16 B `Light` in the same bracket. The adapter's per-DObj material branch table is the only unbounded one, and it already CHECKS before it writes -- so its failure mode is a missing material branch, not corruption, and the overflow counter is structurally blind to it because the pointer never passes `end`. That refusal is now counted (`gNdsTaskmanGraphicsHeapNoRoomCount`, `src/port/diagnostics.c`) and asserted at 0 by the stress harness. 0x2000 is 85x the measured peak and 17x the afterimage bound. **LEVER 2: THE BATTLEPACK CARVE BECOMES A PER-MATCH DECISION (+287,936 B), AND THIS IS THE ONE THAT SCALES.** `battlepack_fox.bin` is ONE fighter's prebuilt clips. On the two-fighter shipping arm it is worth -34,304 ticks; on four distinct kinds P2-3r13 measured it at **-64 ticks at P50**, because it serves one fighter in four and the other three take the on-demand path either way -- for the same 287,936 B of the arena those four kinds have to fit in. `ndsR2BattlePackCarveWorthIt` (`src/port/reloc_backend_assets.c`) now carves only when the packed kind is actually in the match AND the match has at most two distinct kinds; the reservation is lazy (first pack preload step or first cache store), and `gSCManagerBattleState` is filled by `ndsMatchConfigApply` long before `scVSBattleStartBattle`, so the roster is settled at the decision. Declining costs the packed fighter its prebuilt clips and nothing else -- **every path in this cache degrades to the on-demand load, so it is a performance outcome and never a correctness one.** The predicate is asked in `ndsBattlePackResidencyStep` BEFORE the lazy reserve, not after: on the first step the arena does not exist yet, so the existing RawOnly flag reads FALSE and the loader would have streamed 287,904 B of blob into a 163,840 B block. **MEASURED, SAME PROBE, SAME ROM SHAPE:** free at battle frame 2 goes **HALT -> 47,348 B (graphics only) -> 335,284 B (both)**, and both deltas are exact -- +90,112 and +287,936, no rounding. Evidence: `2026-08-25_p2-3f9-shell-argmax-gfxtrim.txt`, `2026-08-25_p2-3f9-shell-argmax-bigalloc.txt`, `2026-08-25_p2-3f9-shell-argmax-packcarve.txt`. **THE GATE, WHOLE-MATCH, AND THE MODEL CLOSES TO THE BYTE.** `p2_fourcpu_stress` on the four-kind roster (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f9-fourcpu-stress2.log`, ROM `833E7845...DA37`, exit 0): frames 1..1973, guest clock 60 -> 1 (98.33%), 0 humans / 4 CPUs / 4 fighter GObjs / mask `0xF`, 0 slips, `syMallocOverflow` 0, `objmanPanic` 0, `poseBindFull` 0 -- and **general-heap low-water 419,052 B against the 25,600 B floor, a margin of 393,452 B**, against P2-3r15's 49,196 B / 23,596 B on the same roster. That is **+369,856 B**, and every term is a constant: 90,112 (graphics heap) + 287,936 (pack carve) = 378,048 returned, less **8,192** of arena the row's own extra code costs (`arenaChosenBytes` 1,695,744 -> 1,687,552, AllocFail 9 -> 11, two more 4,096 steps). 378,048 - 8,192 = 369,856 exactly. **THE TRIM IS NOW PROVEN ON A WHOLE FOUR-KIND MATCH, NOT SAMPLED:** `graphicsHeapCapacityBytes` **8,192**, `graphicsHeapPeakBytes` **96** -- byte-identical to the peak P2-3r13 read at 53,248 -- `graphicsHeapOverflowCount` **0**, `graphicsHeapNoRoomCount` **0**. **AND THE CARVE DECISION IS VISIBLE IN THE LEDGER:** `battlePackResidentBytes` **0**, `battlePackCarveDeclineCount` **1**, `battlePackCarveMatchKinds` **4**, `animCacheArenaReservedBytes` **163,840**. The build flag alone can no longer answer whether the pack was resident, so `battlePackResident` became `battlePackBuilt` plus those four runtime fields. **WHAT THE DECLINE COSTS, MEASURED AND NOT ARGUED.** `ALL` P50/P95 **1,965,184 / 3,085,888** against P2-3r15's 1,964,992 / 3,085,696 -- **192 ticks, flat**, which is r13's -64 prediction holding. `WORK-H` **1,554,432 / 2,811,968** against 1,493,888 / 2,616,384, i.e. **+60,544 P50 and +195,584 P95**, and `animCacheRejects` **279** (misses 283) against 132/143. So the un-packed work is real and it currently fits inside the same VBlank quanta -- report both numbers, because `ALL` is quantized and has hidden a real change before. **The measured next lever falls straight out of it:** when the carve is declined, the raw cache still gets only 163,840 B while 287,936 B goes back to the arena; handing the raw cache part of that would buy those rejects back at a fraction of the bytes. Not taken here -- this row needed the arena, and the rejects each degrade to an on-demand load, never to a wrong frame. **THE STRESS ARM IS RE-POINTED TO THE ACTUAL ARGMAX, which is what `PROJECT_GOAL.md` has always asked for.** `p2_fourcpu_stress` slot 0 leaves Mario (54,048 B, the cheapest of the five) for Captain Falcon (100,160 B), so the arm runs **Captain/Fox/Luigi/Donkey = 335,824 B** of unique per-kind arena instead of Mario/Fox/Luigi/Donkey's 289,712 B. That is a THIRD gate population in three days (mirror -> four landed kinds at P2-3r15 -> argmax here): never compare a figure across it without naming the roster on both sides, and the harness throws if the observed roster disagrees with the build's own flag. Two ledger fields were renamed with it -- `nativeOwnerMario/FoxHardwareTriangles` are SLOT 0/1 totals and slot 0 is no longer Mario, and `battlePackResident` (a build flag) became `battlePackBuilt` plus the runtime `battlePackResidentBytes` / `battlePackCarveDeclineCount` / `battlePackCarveMatchKinds`, because the build flag stopped being able to answer whether the pack was resident the moment the carve became a decision. **METHOD, AND IT COST THE FIRST DESIGN OF THIS ROW'S PROBE.** The first version drove the roster with a gdb poke: it wrote the character select's own `sCssFkind`/`sCssPkind`/`sCssSelected`/`sCssLevel` arrays at the `ndsMenuShellCssCommit` breakpoint, as ALIGNED 32-bit stores (the P2-3r14 one-byte trap avoided by construction), and read the new values straight back -- and the commit one instruction later published the OLD roster (`s0=0x000001 s1=0x010102 s2=0x1c0201`). melonDS's GDB stub reads and writes main RAM BEHIND the ARM9 data cache, so a poke into a line the guest is actively using is invisible to the guest and is then overwritten by the writeback. **This is the write-side of the known read-side trap** ("gdb stack reads are stale on the cache fork", 2026-08-22) and it applies to GLOBALS too, not just stacks: at a real overflow halt this row read `gNdsSyMallocOverflowCount=0 request=0 headroom=0 caller_lr=0` while the same stop's backtrace read `syMallocSet(size=12140, alignment=16)`, and `gNdsTaskmanGeneralHeapFreeMin` read its 0xffffffff initialiser after two battle frames had certainly updated it. **A poke only sticks where the CPU has not touched the line yet** -- which is why `gNdsMenuShellWalkBudget` works and per-frame screen state does not. **Make the guest's own code write the value: that is a build flag, not a breakpoint.** `probe-arena-overflow.ps1` now carries the same warning, because its whole design is reading those published globals and a row of zeroes there means "unreadable", never "no overflow". **Second trap:** `break syTaskmanMalloc` resolves to **20 locations** (inlined into most callers) where the parameter `size` is not in scope, and the capture dies mid-printf on the first hit; `syMallocSet` is the port's out-of-line wrapper with named `(bp, size, alignment)` and exactly one location. **HEADROOM LEFT FOR FIGHTER #6:** the argmax only changes if #6 costs more than Luigi's 41,552 B, and the new margin absorbs a kind far heavier than any landed one. **WHAT WAS NOT TAKEN, and it is still the right next lever if this ever gets tight again:** P2-3r13's option (b), shrinking the per-kind main-file trees (model files 149,616 B / 51.6%, ShieldPose 31,920 B / 11.0%, Fox's ExternDataBank109 47,120 B / 16.3%). Nothing here proves those bytes dead on DS and this row did not need them; ExternDataBank109 in particular is a RELOCATION DEPENDENCY of Fox's own files (`ndsFighterMarioFoxDependencyBit`), so removing it is not a delete but a pointer-graph question. **THE RE-POINTING WAS ATTEMPTED AND BACKED OUT, AND WHAT IT FOUND IS BIGGER THAN THE ROW.** `p2_fourcpu_stress` slot 0 was moved Mario -> Captain to make the arm the real argmax (335,824 B instead of 289,712 B). The arm then **timed out at its 3600 s ceiling having never reached ring stop 0 (target frame 98)**. It is not a stall and not the memory work: bisection by breakpoint showed all four fighters created and battle frames advancing, and **every memory counter matched the control exactly** -- graphics-heap peak 96 B of 8,192, overflow 0, no-room 0, anim-cache misses 4 / rejects 0, and `gNdsBattlePackHits` **0 on BOTH arms**, so the pack was buying nothing there either. What differs is wall clock: **100 stops at `ifCommonBattleUpdateInterfaceAll` reached presented frame 45 in 240 s, against frame 49 in 8 s on the pre-change four-kind ROM** (`build-p2-fourcpu-roster4`) -- about **30x** for the same guest frames. **The control that settles it:** rebuilding this arm with Captain removed but EVERY P2-3f9 memory change still active (carve declined, `carveMatchKinds` 4, `animCacheArenaReservedBytes` 163,840, graphics heap 8,192) reaches **frame 49 in 10 s**. So the 30x is CAPTAIN FALCON IN A FOUR-FIGHTER MATCH, not the arena work, and it is unattributed beyond that -- his own row, with the entry-point census (`gNdsFtrPlanBuild`/`Hit` and the slot triangle mask read 0 at frame 45 on BOTH arms, so they are not the instrument for it). **Consequence to state plainly: the argmax roster no longer HANGS, and it is not yet PLAYABLE.** Memory was one of the two things wrong with it. Evidence: `2026-08-25_p2-3f9-fourcpu-trace.txt`, `...-cache-trace.txt`, `...-gfxheap.txt`, `...-sparse-trace.txt`, `...-frames-trace.txt`, `2026-08-25_p2-3f9-baseline-r13-frames.txt`, `...-baseline-r13-cache.txt`, `...-fourcpu-revert-frames.txt`. **HEADROOM FOR FIGHTER #6:** 393,452 B of measured margin on the stress arm. The argmax only moves when a new kind costs more than Luigi's 41,552 B, and the heaviest landed kind is Fox at 116,752 -- so a Fox-weight #6 debits 75,200 B and three more of them still fit. **The honest gap: the SHELL argmax roster's whole-match low-water is NOT measured** -- only its battle-entry free (335,284 B) -- because at Captain's current cost that match would take hours of wall time. Re-measure it when his row lands. **PUBLISHED:** `smash64ds.nds` **17,644,544 B, SHA-256 `2FB213CC...CA74`** (was `C7FF35D7...E171`, the ROM that hangs), asserted from `builds/build/nds_build_config.h`: `NDS_P2_CAPTAIN 1`, `NDS_P2_MENU_WALK 0`, `NDS_P2_FOUR_CPU_ROSTER 0`, `NDS_P2_SHELL_ARGMAX_ROSTER 0`. The hang proof is on the flag-identical walk build, since the published ROM takes human input and cannot be scripted. |
| P2-3f10 | Captain Falcon makes a four-fighter match ~30x slower — P2-3f9's parting finding, attributed but unfixed | **FIXED 2026-08-25, AND IT WAS NEVER A SLOWDOWN.** It is an unhandled ARM9 data abort in Falcon's entry effect, about 1.9 s into every match he is in — a 1v1 included. The stress arm is re-pointed to the real argmax, he costs nothing over Mario, and Boundary is green on all three arms. | **THE SHAPE ANSWERED A DIFFERENT QUESTION THAN THE ONE ASKED.** The brief asked per-fighter or combinatorial; the two-fighter arm (`smash64ds-battle-playable-tickhud-hwtri` with `NDS_P2_CAPTAIN=1 NDS_P2_PROOF_FIGHTER0=7`, `build-p2-3f10-cap1v1`) **freezes**: the tick HUD stops with `n:45`, the match clock stops at `01:00`, three captures 170 s apart are byte-identical, and melonDS's own title bar falls from **`[132/60]` to `[1/60]`**. A dead guest and a slow guest look the same through a gdb-stop wall clock — P2-3f9's "100 stops reached presented frame 45 in 240 s" was the emulator grinding a wandering ARM9 through zeroed RAM, which is also why every memory counter it read matched the control exactly. **Rail: read the emulator's host-FPS readout before ratioing gdb stop times; a host collapse with a frozen guest is a crash, not a cost.** **THE ABORT, EXACTLY.** Breaking `__excpt_entry` catches it at presented frame 56: `cpsr=0x60000097` — mode `0b10111`, ABORT — and `lr=0x020b4e80`. That `lr` is **the faulting address, not a return address**: `__arm_excpt_dabt` (`0x01fffec0`) does `sub lr, lr, #8` before falling into `__excpt_entry`, so gdb's `#1 … in efManagerCaptainEntryCarMakeEffect` frame is reconstructed from a value that is already the fault PC. `0x020b4e80` disassembles to `ldr r3, [r4, #16]`, the first load of `node_dobj = dobj->child->child->child` (`efmanager.c:5639`), with `r4 = DObjGetStruct(effect_gobj) = NULL`. **A TABLE SIZED FOR A ROSTER THAT NO LONGER EXISTS.** `ndsEFManagerResolveDescOffsets` neutralises an effect desc whose file is not resident when `efManagerInitEffects` sweeps (`proc_display = NULL`), records it in `sNdsEFDeferredDescs`, and `ndsEFManagerRetryDeferredDescs` restores it once the file arrives — `ftCommonAppearSetStatus` calls that retry immediately before the source entry effect for exactly this reason. `NDS_EF_DEFERRED_MAX` was **4**, and its own comment says why: it was sized when the deferrable set was Mario's pipe, Fox's Arwing and Fox's reflector, "with one spare". The landed roster defers **seven** — those three plus DK's barrel plus Falcon's Flyer, Kick and Punch. Measured at Falcon's entry (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f10-captain-car-desc.txt`): **`EFDESC disabled=7 unknownfile=7 recover=2 overflow=3`, `CARDESC proc=(nil)`**, with `special2=0x22efea0` resident and the anim-joint table readable at `+0x6200` — nothing was missing except a slot in a four-entry array. **A desc the table cannot remember can never be re-enabled**, and `efManagerMakeEffect` answers a neutralised desc with a bare GObj carrying **no DObj tree** (`efmanager.c:1971`). The file's own comment claims that is safe because "the guarded accessors downstream see a NULL DObjGetStruct and return cleanly" — **the source makers do not honour that contract**: Falcon's walks three children deep on the very next line and DK's writes `dobj->translate` on its next line, so a lost slot is a data abort, not a missing effect. **THE FIX IS THE BOUND, NOT THE NUMBER.** The per-roster descs are now an X-list (`NDS_EF_ROSTER_DESCS`, DK + Falcon's three) beside the existing `NDS_EF_MANAGER_DESCS`; `ndsEFManagerResolveAllDescOffsets` expands both instead of naming Falcon's three by hand (net −16 lines); and a `_Static_assert` counts both lists against `NDS_EF_DEFERRED_MAX`, now 24. Fighter #6 adds one line to one list, and forgetting the size is a **build error** rather than a silent abort eight hours later. After: `EFDESC disabled=7 unknownfile=7 recover=6 overflow=0` and the four-kind match runs (`…-captain-car-fixed.txt`). Cost: 160 B of `.bss`. **HE COSTS NOTHING OVER MARIO.** Argmax (Captain/Fox/Luigi/Donkey) against the pre-change four-kind control (`build-p2-fourcpu-tickhud`, Mario/Fox/Luigi/Donkey), same target, same capture delays, same stage: FPS **5.6 vs 5.4**, `ALL` **5,598,464 / 6,719,680 vs 5,599,104 / 6,720,128**, match clock **00:50 vs 00:51** at t+85 s, all four slots drawing. Indistinguishable — so P2-3f9's "the argmax roster is affordable but not playable" was half right: the memory was the only thing wrong with it, and the other half was this crash. Screens: `artifacts/visibility/2026-08-25_p2-3f10-cap4kind-{a,b}.png` and `…-ctl4kind-{a,b}.png`. **THE STRESS ARM RE-POINT WAS ATTEMPTED TWICE AND IS BACKED OUT AGAIN — for a NEW reason, and the old one is dead.** P2-3f9's blocker (the "30x") is fixed above. Two builds were then measured: Captain in slot 0 (`build-p2-3f10-cap4kind`) and Captain in slot 2 (`build-p2-3f10-argmax2`). **Both make the slot-3 fighter stop emitting hardware triangles for the whole match** — `gNdsFighterDLAllDrawSlotTriangleMask` **0x7**, which is exactly the assertion `verify-p2-four-fighter-stress.ps1:465` throws on, and the one instrument P2-3r15 added because the P0/P1 triangle totals cannot speak for slots 2 and 3. That is row **P2-3f11**; this row leaves `p2_fourcpu_stress` on Mario/Fox/Luigi/Donkey so Boundary stays green, and records what it eliminated so the next attempt starts past it. **AND THE ARGMAX ITSELF WAS WRONG, in both earlier rows.** P2-3f8 and P2-3f9 say "Mario is the cheapest of the five and drops out" and derive `Fox+Captain+Donkey+Luigi = 335,824 B`. Read the per-kind table they both print: Luigi is **41,552 B**, Mario is **54,048 B**. Luigi is the cheapest by 12,496 B, so the argmax over landed content is **Mario/Fox/Captain/Donkey = 348,320 B** — heavier than the roster those rows proposed, and it is Luigi who drops out. Every "the argmax is 335,824 B" statement in the tree is off by that much. **WHAT THIS ROW DID NOT FIX, and it is bigger than this row: the four-fighter arm presents at ~5 FPS on BOTH rosters** — 12 VBlanks per presented frame, its own histogram reading `5+:465 max:21`, the match clock advancing 7 guest seconds in 40 s of wall clock while melonDS holds `[60/60]`. That is pre-existing P2-2 debt, not Falcon's, and the gate has been under-reporting it. Row **P2-2p7**. |

Timing printed by this run is NOT banked because P2-2p7's known 2^22 sampler correction still falsifies long honest frames. After all temporary P2-3f11 probes were removed, `2026-08-26_p2-3f11-postprobe.json` independently reaches frame 129 with **slot mask 0xF**. With no arm in `ndsRelocNormalizeFighterAttributesFile` he loaded `dead_fgm_ids = {292, 427}` (source: `{427, 292}`), `deadup_sfx = 422` / `damage_sfx = 420` (source: 420 / 422), `smash_sfx = {417, 416, 0}` (source: `{416, 417, 418}`), `heavyget_sfx = 0` (source: 426); `itemthrow_vel/damage_scale` are 100/100 so their swap is an identity. His smash triple IS the ordinary Smash1..3, unlike Captain Falcon's `{Smash3, Smash2, JumpAerial}`: neither fighter's arm can be derived from the other's *shape* either. **WHAT IT WAS COSTING, from the reader census on the LINKED ELF of the ROM the owner plays** (not from `src/`): `ftCommonDeadInitStatusVars` 0x020a1bd0 reads 0xb4/0xb6, `ftCommonDeadUpStarSetStatus` 0x020a214e reads 0xb8, `ndsBaseFTCommonDamageInitDamageVars` 0x0208f506 reads 0xba, `ftMainParseMotionEvent` 0x020bb650 reads `smash_sfx[syUtilsRandIntRange(3)]` at 0xbc. `itemthrow_*` has **no** reader (items are P2-5; `it/itmain.c` is not imported) and 0xe8's only reader is the validator itself. The time bomb is `heavyget_sfx == 0`, which **passes** the source's `!= nSYAudioFGMVoiceEnd` (695) guard, so once `it/itmain.c` lands a heavy pickup requests id 0 instead of skipping. And the moment Luigi's bank IS packed, every lane above would have played the wrong cue — his damage voice would have been his star-KO voice. **THE ARM, ON THE ELF.** `ndsRelocFighterAttributesMatchSource` is inlined into `ndsRelocFinalizeLoadedFile`; the Luigi block is at **0x02041bc0** and all ten constants reconstruct from its Thumb immediates (427, 292, 420, 422, 416, 417, 418, 100, 100, 426). That generator is `check-fighter-production-manifest.ps1`, which runs in **every** `verify-all` profile, so fighter #6 landing without an arm is a red gate rather than a silent lane reversal. `check-audio-runtime-fixtures.ps1` gained the value half: it fixtured Mario and Fox only, and now fixtures all five (2 -> 5 FTAttributes blocks) plus their five `ftdata.c` offsets. |

Honest bound: `ring=16` is the ring's own capacity, so "no Captain id" is a FLOOR, not a census. **THE SAME RUN SETTLED TWO THINGS THAT ARE NOT HIS.** It confirms **P2-3f12 at runtime**: the ring names 422 `nSYAudioVoiceLuigiDamage` three times, and 422 is the POST-normalizer value of `attr->damage_sfx` — before that arm the same field read 420 and the ring would have named 420; no phantom id 0 appears either. And it names the next two audio rows, measured rather than guessed: **Donkey Kong's FGM bank is entirely unpacked** (9/72/175/176/177/178/179/298 — he got his VOICE bank at P2-3 and never his sound effects) and **Luigi's voices are too** (422, 427). Both are louder than anything left in Falcon's. **THE PROBE ITSELF HAD TO BE FIXED TWICE TO ANSWER THIS.** `probe-p2-shell.ps1` printed FOUR of the ring's sixteen slots, and the first four could not say which sixteen ids a four-fighter match had missed — a ring that reports a quarter of itself cannot answer "is this fighter's bank complete", which is the question a fighter landing asks it. And its default artifact path was DATE-ONLY, so two probes of different configurations on the same day overwrote each other: an argmax run landed on top of P2-3f8's `build-p2-shell` residency capture and there is no undo, because `artifacts/` is untracked (P2-3f8's figures survive quoted in falcon.md). The default now carries the BUILD. **THREE CHECKER DEFECTS FOUND ON THE WAY, ALL FIXED AT THEIR OWN SEAM.** (1) `check-audio-fgm-phase-pack.ps1` was **in no verify profile** — hand-run only — and the failure it owns is TOTAL SILENCE; that exact constant drift has shipped twice (2026-08-02 rejected all 88 cues, 2026-08-24 built a 119-entry pack against a runtime compiled for 117). It is 18 s and static, and it now runs in `verify-all.ps1`'s unconditional block (`$expectedVerifiers` 7 → 8). (2) Its allowlist cross-check was a **hand-written list of 28 names** — "the announcer set at least" — which is a list that only ever covers what somebody remembered; it now derives from the pack and requires EVERY packed entry to have a `case` label by name or by bare id. Thirty-four cues landed in one row here and the hand list would have covered one. (3) Its **12,000 Hz frequency floor was a proxy that this row falsified.** The floor existed to catch FGM 488 GAME SET, whose rate came from a leading REST (pitch code 0). Falcon's Flyer engine (180) voices at **10,679 Hz on purpose** — a real pitch code 6 plus a −1,200-cent articulation — and tripped it. The guard now asserts the thing it actually means, recomputing the rate's pitch code from the entry's own program and failing only when a rest set the rate: strictly stronger (it catches 488 at any frequency) and it no longer rejects a cue for being deep. |
| P2-3f14 | Donkey Kong has NO SOUND EFFECTS — he received his VOICE bank at P2-3 and never his FGM cues, so every cue his motion scripts, his `dead_fgm_ids[1]` and the shared DownBounce table ask for falls into the miss ring | **LANDED 2026-08-25. All twelve are packed and proven at runtime: not one Donkey id survives in the miss ring, `fgmfail=0`, and the ring itself fell from SATURATED to a census.** | **THE GAP WAS MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS FIXED.** P2-3f13's argmax shell run counted **90 DK FGM requests in one minute, every one missing**, with `DonkeyCharge` alone asked for **56 times**. **THE INVENTORY IS THE SOURCE'S.** Every `nSYAudio*` enumerator `212_DonkeyMainMotion.c` and `213_DonkeyMain.c` name — 35 of them, `gm/gmsound.h` parsed with REGION_US honored rather than remembered — plus `scsubsysdatadonkey.c` and `ft/ftcommondata.c`'s DownBounce and fighter-call tables. Nine are his own (9 Slap1, 72 Landing, 105 Foot, 116 Dash, 175 BossSlam, 176 BossUnk1, 177 BossUnk2, 178 Spin, 179 Charge); 287 DeadSlam is his `dead_fgm_ids[1]` (`0x11f`) and 298 DownBounce is his row in the shared table. **10 DonkeySlap2 rides with them and is NOT his** — Captain/Kirby/Purin/Yoshi play it and his 175/176 fork it — so packing it also closes one of the seven roster-wide shared cues P2-3f13 left open (that list is now SIX; `falcon.md` is corrected). **The three Boss-named cues are not Giant DK's**: `212_DonkeyMainMotion.c` plays all three from ordinary DK actions. **FOUR OF THE TWELVE WERE ALREADY IN THE PACK AS SOMEBODY ELSE'S FORK TARGET, which is the strongest cross-check this block could have.** 287 is the slam program Mario's 292, Fox's 289 and Captain's 288 all fork; 298 is the DownBounce program 299/300/303 fork; 105 and 116 are the programs Captain's 106 Foot and 117 Dash fork. Their pinned root hashes are byte-for-byte the `render_program_sha256` those entries already carry, and their extents came out of the generator's own validator at exactly **5,168 / 2,301 / 828 / 1,239** — the numbers those entries declare. **EVERY FIELD IS `--derive`, EVERY EXTENT IS THE VALIDATOR'S.** Seven cues render through `FULL_PROGRAM_AOT_IDS` because the flat path holds a cue's FIRST note for its whole duration and 179 DonkeyCharge is pitch codes **13/3/12 in nine ticks** — it would come out monotone. 175/176 fuse their fork of 10, confirmed by the metadata's own `voice_program_ids` reading `[175, 10]` and `[176, 10]` with **zero** omitted forks pack-wide. **BUDGET.** Pack 1,511,844 → **1,551,484 B** (+39,640, cap 2,097,152), entries 153 → **165**, `.bss` +384 B. Largest new body 176 BossUnk1 **10,584 B** against the 53,248-byte largest cache slot; runtime cache unchanged at 204,800 — the pack is streamed, never resident. **VERIFIED ON THE LINKED ELF, NOT ON `src/`** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f/dk-luigi-audio-bank.txt`). **A CHECKER DEFECT FOUND ON THE WAY, FIXED AT ITS SEAM.** `check-audio-id-fixtures.ps1` harvests names from `include/gm/gmsound.h` with a bare identifier regex and **never stripped comments**, so this row's own block comment — "the three `nSYAudioFGMBoss*` cues" — made it emit a `_Static_assert` about `nSYAudioFGMBoss`, an identifier in neither header, and fail the entire fixture. It strips comments now: a checker a COMMENT can break does not check what it claims to. It passes **245** constants where it passed 233, which is the twelve new ordinals confirmed a second, independent way. `check-audio-runtime-fixtures.ps1`'s KO set gains 287 and its attack set gains the seven attack-kind cues; its stale note claiming "nothing has asked for 287 yet" is replaced by what actually happened. |
| P2-3f15 | Luigi's voices are unpacked — he ships selectable with exactly TWO cues, so every voice his `FTAttributes` lanes and motion scripts name fails closed | **LANDED 2026-08-25. His whole source voice run plus his crowd chant are packed, proven at runtime the same way, and nothing Luigi-specific is unpacked after it.** | **THIS IS WHY P2-3f12 WAS LATENT.** That row corrected his mixed-u16 `FTAttributes` lanes and nothing audible changed, because correcting them only changed WHICH unpacked id he asked for. P2-3f13's run named 422 `LuigiDamage` and 427 `LuigiDead` in the miss ring — six requests in one lap. **THE INVENTORY IS THE SOURCE'S:** `gm/gmsound.h`'s contiguous run **416..428** (REGION_US honored by a full enum parse) plus his row in `ft/ftcommondata.c`'s `dFTCommonDataPublicFighterCallFGMs`, **608 PublicLuigi** — the crowd chant no packed cue answered. Reachability is `220_LuigiMainMotion.c` (Smash1, SpecialLw, Jump, JumpAerial, FuraFura), `221_LuigiMain.c`'s attribute block at 0x580, and `scsubsysdataluigi.c`. 421 FuraFura and announcer 498 landed at P2-3 and keep their pack order. **HIS KO SLAM AND DOWN-BOUNCE ARE MARIO'S, NOT MISSING.** `dead_fgm_ids[1]` is `nSYAudioFGMMarioDeadSlam` (292) and the DownBounce table gives him `nSYAudioFGMMarioDownBounce` (303); the source spells both that way and both have been packed since P1. Same trap P2-3f12 recorded about deriving one fighter's arm from another's by name substitution. **RENDER.** All twelve voices are multi-note schedules with no forks and take `FULL_PROGRAM_AOT_IDS` at `duration_ticks * 184`; 608 is a single 330-tick note and renders flat like every other crowd chant. Largest body **420 DeadUp 32,664 B** against the 53,248-byte slot — **nothing here needed `runtime_note_replay` and nothing was refused**, unlike DK's 324 and Falcon's 356. Honest note: 420 is the only fighter DeadUp big enough to need the pack's SINGLE 52 KiB cache slot, which seven crowd cues already contend for; that contention is pre-existing, its symptom would be `fgmfail`, and `fgmfail` measured **0**. **425 `Lets` AND 428 `HereWe` ARE THE TWO THE SOURCE MARKS UNUSED** — 425 has no reference anywhere and 428's only one is `mnsoundtest.c`, a P2-7 screen. Packed anyway at 41,960 B: the run is contiguous in the source, ROM is the cheap resource per `PROJECT_GOAL.md`, and a bank that is complete never has to be reopened. **BUDGET.** Pack 1,551,484 → **1,733,284 B** (+181,800, cap 2,097,152), entries 165 → **178**, `.bss` +416 B, cache unchanged at 204,800; **363,868 B of pack budget left**. `check-audio-id-fixtures` passes **257** constants against BattleShip where it passed 245. **THE RUNTIME PROOF IS ONE CONFIGURATION MEASURED TWICE.** `build-p2-3f13-audio` (before) and `build-p2-3f14-audio` (after) have **byte-identical `nds_build_config.h` apart from the git short hash**, so this is a before/after and not a comparison of two different things. Slot 0 Luigi (human), slot 1 Fox, slot 2 Captain, **slot 3 Donkey**, CPUs at level 3, one scripted lap at fast logic 0 through the whole shell and the real one-minute match. `fgmcalls` is **474 in both runs** — the lap asked for exactly the same cues; only the answers changed. Miss ring **16 → 8**, with **not one Donkey, Luigi or Captain id** left, `fgmfail=0`, `fgmloaded=1`. **`fgmfail=0` IS THE POSITIVE HALF AND IT IS NEW.** `ndsAudioFgmPlayAtPan` sends an unfound cue to the miss ring ONLY when `ndsAudioFgmIDIsIncluded` says FALSE; an allowlisted id with no pack entry bumps `fgmfail` instead (`nds_audio_fgm.c:1362`). These two rows added 25 ids to that allowlist, so `fgmfail=0` proves all 25 resolved — ring absence alone could not, which is exactly why the allowlist must never be read as the gate. **AND THE AFTER RING IS A CENSUS, NOT A FLOOR** (8 against the ring's capacity of 16, so nothing was evicted) — which uncovered two cues the SATURATED before-ring had been evicting: **214 `MarioDokan`, the Mario AND Luigi entry pipe, and 191 `FoxAppearArwing`.** Captain Falcon's Flyer has been packed since P2-3f13, so today the Falcon entry roars and the Mario, Luigi and Fox entries are silent. **The complete remaining roster-wide gap is now eight ids, measured rather than suspected**: 95 GroundGrind1 x3, 84 UnkGrind3 x3, 94 KirbyPurinJump x3, 1 ExplodeL x3, 83 UnkGrind2 x3, 214 MarioDokan x1, 191 FoxAppearArwing x1, 59 ContainerSmash x1 — plus 128, 17, 630, 631, 635 and 110 read out of the source motion arrays but not asked for in this lap. **Falcon's 356 was also SIZED here** and is smaller than it looked: his notes are 13/12/13, only TWO codes one semitone apart, and the envelope point's `flags` byte uses exactly one of its eight bits behind a fail-closed guard — four spare bits carrying a signed semitone offset plus a 16-entry Q16 ratio table would put 356 at **14,284 B instead of 65,324**, in the 16 KiB slot class, with the source note schedule preserved exactly. Full sizing in `falcon.md`. Both remain OPEN and neither was folded into a fighter landing. **BOUNDARY GREEN ON ALL THREE ARMS** (`artifacts/verification/2026-08-25_p2-3f/boundary-p2-3f15.log`): `p2_shell_loop` 1 lap / 11 scene entries / per-kind high-waters flat / free floor 293,572 B / zero faults; `p2_battle_realtime` through the shell with the published-ROM contract passing on the new `smash64ds.nds`; `p2_fourcpu_stress` correctness, cadence, native-owner and memory gates all passed, `gNdsFighterDLAllDrawSlotTriangleMask=15` (0xF, all four slots drawing), heap free low-water **414,956 B**, `slips=0`, zero allocator/object/particle failures. Published ROM rebuilt and pinned: **18,116,608 B, SHA-256 `457F5D0A…C91A`** — board line, `HANDOFF.md` and the file on disk all agree, and the frozen P1 pin is untouched. **One process note worth keeping:** the first Boundary launch went RED on `check-docs` because it was started while `HANDOFF.md` was still 206 lines mid-trim — a gate reads the tree it is given, so launch it only from a settled tree. That red log is banked under a deliberately different name (`midedit-docs-red.log`) so it can never be mistaken for the run that superseded it. |
| P2-3f16 | FOUR OF THE FIVE LANDED FIGHTERS ENTER SILENT — only Captain Falcon's entry has a packed cue, and the inconsistency is in the first second of every match | **LANDED 2026-08-26 (`6d7e3e97797`). ## Queue — P2-2 performance debt (2026-08-23, goal: "<1.12m CPU ticks")

Owner rule reminder: bug-fixing and performance attribution are the orchestrator's;
the subagent switch is OFF. Measured on the four-CPU stress arm, tick-HUD ROM,
`scripts/verify-p2-four-fighter-stress.ps1` (`-Build` for the lab A/B).

| ID | Slice | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| P2-2p1 | Four-CPU performance debt: fighter GX packet replay | **PROMOTED to default-on 2026-08-23 — Boundary GREEN on the promoted tree (commit `3d0fcfff316`), published `smash64ds.nds` carries it (sha256 `3adb9db0e7821b3d…`, 16,314,368 B); owner visual pass pending** | The goal names "<1.12m ticks"; the four-CPU arm sat at `WORK-H` P50/P95 1,600,832 / 2,069,824 on the current tree. Sizing first: `NDS_R2_DRAW_SUPPRESS_MASK=15` (no fighter draws) reads 993,792 / 1,462,144, so the four fighter draws are **607,040 ticks at P50** and the renderer's fighter side is the lever; the profile partition (`task81_partition_census.py` on the 2026-08-22 four-CPU profile) puts work at 1,788K/frame with a 668K gap, 60–80% stall in every class. The cut: `NDS_R2_FIGHTER_PACKET=1` captures each fighter's whole GX command stream once as packed GXFIFO words (the stage replay's format) and DMAs it every frame, patching only the projection, each root's seed/joint-chain matrices and the light vector; the words borrow `gSYFramebufferSets` (read only by the Results photo wipe, which `ndsRendererFighterPacketRelease` restores on Results entry). Same target, same tree: **`WORK-H` 1,415,552 / 2,022,144 (−185,280 P50), FTR lane 665,920 → 414,528**, 2-VBlank frames 68 → 151, all gate checks green, hardware triangle counters equal to the baseline, top-screen captures pixel-identical at entry. Packet probe: ~3.3 hits and 0.6 re-records per frame, 0 faults, largest packet 2,221 words. ITCM was full: record helpers are cold/noinline main RAM, the matrix-loader tee is a main-RAM twin, and four bottom-of-rent residents (`SetParticleCamera`, `MtxLoadN64ToDS20p12`, `RecordSetTileSize`, `RecordLoadBlock`) are evicted in packet builds only. Lab A/B surface: `verify-p2-four-fighter-stress.ps1 -Build`. Commit `75d8b95bf9e`. Promotion found one accounting hole: a replay hit presented the fighters' batches by DMA but the frame-summary batch/texture-prepare counters never saw them (`RENDER_BATCH=54,707,54,0,707` against the loop harness's `103,725,103,49,725`), so the packet now stores the record frame's presented-work deltas — batch begin/reuse/end, texture prepare/reuse (net of the raw/cross credits), matrix loads, texture binds, source vertex loads (`RENDER_LAZY[0]` had read 0 against a `-gt 0` liveness contract) — and every hit adds them back; the counters keep meaning "what the hardware was handed" and no harness needs packet knowledge. CPU-work counters (uploads, lookups, near-plane rejects, saturates) are deliberately not credited: a hit does none of that work. The second failure was found offline by diffing the terminal `KEY=` dump against the last green gate's (`builds/p2-1m-gate3.log`) instead of spending another Boundary. |
| P2-2p2 | Packet re-record churn | **lab KEEP, measured 2026-08-23** (commit `1357d479d2d`) | Miss-word census (480 frames): 222 of 350 re-records lined up with hurt-flash draws (colour modulate + root prim), ~75 with root-set/chain-shape changes (status transitions — legitimate), ~24 with texture-cache generation bumps (which re-recorded all four packets at once — the FTR P95 rise 711,680 → 751,424). Fix: DIF_AMB words are per-site tint patches re-derived on replay from the recorded light colours and the live prim/modulate (`ndsFighterPacketApplyTint`, with `prim_from_root` tracking material/state-delta prim overrides), and texture residency is validated per packet against the cache entries it bound instead of the global fence. After: re-records 0.73 → 0.17/frame (all remaining = root set changing on status transitions), **`WORK-H` 1,356,736 / 1,927,296, FTR 391,808 / 570,624** (FTR P95 now under the baseline's 711,680), 4-VBlank frames 766 → 359, gate green. Cumulative vs the baseline: −244,096 P50 / −142,528 P95. |
| P2-2p3 | Packet DMA overlap | **lab KEEP, measured 2026-08-23** | The replay no longer waits for its own DMA; the next FIFO writer waits first (`ndsFighterPacketDmaWait` at the production execute entry, stage owner prepare and segment commit, particle/Whispy/blaster/gun/impact-wave/halo/entry-effect submits, the generic display-list executors, and `ndsPlatformEndFrame` before `glFlush`). Same arm: **`WORK-H` 1,312,384 / 1,870,080, FTR 366,784 / 552,064**, 4-VBlank frames 359 → 288, stream counters identical to p2 (0 faults), gate green. Cumulative vs the baseline: −288,448 P50 / −199,744 P95. A writer that bypasses those seams would interleave its words with the DMA's — the seam list is the contract. |
| P2-2p4 | Packet hit-path adapter trimming | **lab KEEP, measured 2026-08-23** | The packet-on profile census (`artifacts/performance/2026-08-23_p2-2-fourcpu-profile128-packet`, line profile of `ndsFighterMarioFoxDLAllDrawForSlot`) put the material rows (20.6K), prepare (10.1K), snapshots (16K) and validation on every frame although a replay reads none of them. The adapter now takes the packet key's material half from the live MObj chains alone (`ndsRendererAdapterMaterialIdentity`), builds the production inputs, and asks `ndsRendererFighterPacketPrecheck` — the replay's own predicate on the same inputs — before preparing materials; a predicted hit skips the rows/snapshots/validation outright. Same arm: **`WORK-H` 1,281,728 / 1,866,432, FTR 306,560 / 516,928**, material snapshot builds 31 → 0.5/frame, 2-VBlank frames 177 → 294, stream counters identical, gate green. Cumulative vs the baseline: **−319,104 P50 / −203,392 P95**. **Visual evidence:** the deterministic entry series on the four-CPU ROM, baseline vs packet, is pixel-identical over the emulator content at +8/+40/+72/+136 logic frames (`artifacts/visibility/2026-08-23_packet-{base,on}-fighter-entry-1{,+40,+72,+136}.png`, 0 differing top-screen pixels in all four pairs). Mid-match cross-ROM captures are NOT evidence: two runs of the same baseline ROM differ by 107K pixels at frame 300 (CPU matches are not run-to-run deterministic). Remaining on the fighter lane after p4 (per the census): joint-matrix math ~65K, chain capture 15K, production inputs 15K, the replay's own patch/flush ~48K, tick-HUD instruments ~20K (absent from the shipped ROM). The remaining four-CPU gap is the source lanes (SRC 594K P50 / 1,033K P95, OTHR 360K), which the plan parks behind the sacrifice order pending owner approval. Free-play twin with the packet on: `builds/build-p2-shell-freeplay-packet/smash64ds-p2-shell-freeplay-hwtri.nds`. |
